| `network` | GeoDNS detection; China mirror router |
| `notify` | LISTEN/NOTIFY dispatcher → WebSocket fan-out; welcome/system email sender. `external.go` exports `BuildSystemMIME` and `SendSystemMail`, the shared RFC 5322 renderer + MinIO/`outbound_queue` helper used by booking, guest invitations, and guest digests. `SendSystemMail` accepts the exported Exec-only `SystemMailQueue` seam, satisfied by both `*pgxpool.Pool` and `pgx.Tx`, so a caller may make the queue insert atomic with its own state transition. `SetTestSender` is a serialized, restore-closure test seam; callers must restore it and must not nest overrides. |
| `pushjobs` | Mail-push worker (V107, QoL #9) **+ chat mobile-push drain leg (chat A3.5)**. `NewWorker(workerPool, pushSet, logger).Run(ctx)` — on `workerPool` (BYPASSRLS; both queues + `push_devices` are cross-tenant). **Mail leg** (3s ticker): drains `push_intents` (enqueued by `deliver()` when `feature.mail_push` is ON, not-spam, not-muted). Per intent: claim (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, lease via `next_attempt_at = now()+2m`, `attempts++` — no `processing` status) → `push_should_notify` gate (prefs/VIP/DND/mute) → build rich payload from the message → `shared/push.ResolveDevices` → `Send` per device. `ErrUnregistered` ⇒ prune (`revoked_at`); any-success or all-pruned ⇒ `done`; all-retryable-failed ⇒ retry with capped exponential backoff (`LEAST(1m·2^attempts, 1h)`, copied from `scheduled_actions`), `failed` after 5 attempts. `pushSet` empty ⇒ still drains (marks done). Test: `worker_test.go` (`evalSends` gate/prune/partial-success + `backoffFor`, fake dispatcher, no DB). **Chat leg** (`chat.go`, runs every tick right after the mail leg drains empty — a mail backlog can delay chat by one tick, accepted for a rider phase): drains `chat_push_intents` — **one row per MESSAGE** (`chat_message_send` enqueues it when `feature.chat_push` is ON, V124), never per member; the worker fans out per member AT DRAIN TIME via `chat_push_should_notify(user_id, channel_id, message_id)` (the chat sibling of `push_should_notify` — same DND-window logic, chat's own `notif_level`/`chat_notifications`/self/deleted checks). `claimOn`/`markDoneOn`/`retryOrFailOn` are shared, table-parametrized helpers (`pushIntentsTable`/`chatIntentsTable`, both fixed Go constants — allowlisted in `check-sql-injection.sh`, never request-derived) so the mail and chat legs reuse one claim/done/retry implementation instead of two copies. **Retry policy differs from mail**: once per-member fan-out begins the intent is ALWAYS terminal-`done` regardless of individual send outcomes — re-fanning on one flaky device would re-push every member who already succeeded (no cross-provider tag-replace-on-retry); push is a best-effort wake hint. **Mention-safe coalescing**: an older `pending` intent on the same channel is skipped as `done` when a newer `pending` one exists, UNLESS the older message carries a `chat_mentions` row or is `broadcast` (a 'mentions'-level member's push must never silently die with a coalesced message). **Payload carries no message body** (payload-privacy posture — chat starts stricter than mail's rich payload, a registered `push.content_level` unification deferral): `{type:"chat",channelId,messageId,seq,sender,channelName,mention,dedupeTag:"chat-<channelId>"}`, built PER MEMBER (unlike mail's one-payload-per-intent) because DM/group-DM `channelName` differs per recipient (`chatChannelName`: channel→its name, DM→`""` for client-side i18n, group-DM→other members' names). A separate reaper (`reapChatIntents`, ticks alongside the chat leg) deletes terminal (`done`/`failed`) `chat_push_intents` rows older than 7 days — nothing reaps mail's `push_intents` either (a pre-existing gap, noted as a backport candidate, not fixed here); the 7-day interval is the A4 seam (a future push-emission audit record would change it). Test: `chat_test.go` (`shouldCoalesce`/`planChatFanout`/`chatChannelName` pure-function units, DB-free, + `TestChatPushDrainE2E` — a real DB-gated live webpush round trip: `chat_message_send` → real `chat_push_should_notify` gate → real RFC 8291 encrypted no-body dispatch → muted member gets zero, notified member gets exactly one → intent `done`/`attempts=1` with no retry after fan-out). **Guest G1 leg** (`guest.go`, unconditional after chat): drains `guest_notification_intents` with active-session skip, configured throttle/50-row batch, current-authorization rendering, disabled-guest tombstones, and a 7-day sent/stale reaper; `feature.guest_accounts` gates enqueue only. |
| `notifyjobs` | NOTIFY-decouple drain worker (V088). `NewWorker(workerPool, logger).Run(ctx)` — 1s ticker; each `drainOnce` DELETEs up to 500 `notify_intents` rows and fires their `pg_notify` in ONE short txn (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, at-most-once, id order). Intents are written by the `app_notify` reducer only when `feature.notify_decouple` is ON (seeded OFF by V088) — toggle OFF ⇒ empty table, no-op ticks, byte-identical direct-notify behavior (proof: `sql/tests/notify_decouple.pg`). Runs unconditionally so residue drains after a flip-off. Near-copy of `eapjobs.Worker`. Test: `worker_test.go` (drain fires the notification on a real LISTEN conn + empties the queue). |

| `reducercall` | The shared reducer-call seam: a `Caller` interface (`Call`/`CallScoped` → `BuildReducerSQL` → `pool.QueryRow` + Scan) + `PoolCaller` impl, lifted out of `jmap` so every protocol (jmap, carddav, caldav) invokes reducers through one **fake-able** seam (a fake `Caller` returns canned JSON/errors in a unit test, no DB). `jmap`'s `reducerCaller` is now a thin alias over it. **c5 — scope == $1 by construction:** `CallScoped(ctx, mailboxID, fn, args...)` both sets the tx-scoped `app.current_mailbox_id` GUC AND prepends `mailboxID` as the reducer's `$1` itself — call sites state the tenant id ONCE and pass only `$2…` as `args` (the pre-c5 double-pass, GUC arg + repeated `$1`, is gone from all ~59 jmap sites). A reducer whose `$1` is not the caller's tenant scope must use plain `Call` (the worker-pool cross-tenant legs already do); no such scoped consumer exists, so there is deliberately no `CallScopedAs` escape hatch. Invariant pinned by `TestScopedShape_PrependsScopeAsArg1` (no-DB, byte-identical SQL/arg-shape) + the DB-backed `TestCallScoped_SetsGUCToAuthenticatedMailbox` (probe returns GUC and `$1`, asserts equal). **c3 — one JMAP /set = one transaction:** `CallScopedBatch(ctx, mailboxID, calls ...ScopedCall)` runs N reducer calls inside ONE `BEGIN → set_config (once, is_local) → fnᵢ($1=scope,…) → COMMIT` — scope stated once for the whole batch, GUC == every leg's `$1` by construction, any error rolls back ALL legs. jmap `dispatchSet` routes create/update/destroy through one batch (RFC 8620 §3.6.2 all-or-nothing: a mid-batch infra failure no longer leaves creates committed; per-ITEM failures stay item-level via the set reducers' internal EXCEPTION handlers = implicit savepoints, which work unchanged inside the explicit tx). Pinned by `TestCallScopedBatch_OneTxGUCOnceAcrossAllPhases` (same txid + GUC==`$1` per leg), `…_AllOrNothing_RollsBackEarlierPhases`, `…_ItemLevelFailureDoesNotAbortBatch`, `…_GUCDoesNotLeakAcrossTransactions` (PgBouncer tx-mode), mirrored through jmap's shim in `caller_scoped_test.go`; DB-side rollback proof in `sql/tests/jmap_email.pg` (tests 35–36). **Observability (TODOS “Reducer observability”):** every `PoolCaller` invocation (`Call`, `CallScoped`, each `CallScopedBatch` leg) records reducer name + SQLSTATE (`pgconn.PgError`) + duration via structured slog — WARN for classified BZ errors and successes over `SlowThreshold` (500ms package var), ERROR for unclassified errors, silent on fast success; errors pass through untouched (`errors.As` preserved). Log-only (the Prometheus `metrics.Handler` is instance-wired in main.go; feed `RecordMethod` via an optional recorder field when the construction sites are in scope). `Logger` field on `PoolCaller` (nil → `slog.Default()`) is the test seam — pinned by the six `TestObserve_*` in `observe_test.go`. |
| ~~`reducererr`~~ | **Moved to `shared/reducererr` (arch #A8)** so authserver (a separate Go module) can consume the same BZ classification instead of hand-rolling its own `pgErr.Code` branches (`terms.go`/`auth.go`, now converted to `Classify`/`Detail`/`IsCode`) — the two `check-reducererr-contract.sh` allowlist entries for those files are deleted. Every mailserver consumer (jmap/smtp/caldav/carddav/scim/admin/user/chat/meet/drive) now imports `setjoin.com/shared/reducererr`; contract + symbols unchanged. Package summary: leaf owning the BZ business-error vocabulary (`Err*` constants, `ReducerError`, `ParseReducerError`, `CodeIsKnown`) per `docs/reducer-error-codes.md`; depends only on `pgx`/`pgconn` and `shared/httputil` (both leaves) so every protocol layer routes on the stable BZ code without an import cycle. (The `admin.*` re-export shim — `admin/reducer_errors.go` — was a zero-non-test-consumer dead alias, deleted arch review #A5; `admin` now imports `reducererr` directly like every other consumer.) **`wire.go` — the shared reducer→HTTP translator kit (whole-pipeline review arch #3):** `WriteProblem(w, r, err, ProblemOptions{Namespace, Slugs, Override, Extend})` is the ONE home for what `chat/errors.go`'s `writeChatProblem`, `meet/errors.go`'s `writeMeetProblem`, and `drive/problem.go`'s `writeDriveProblem` each hand-rolled (byte-identical between chat/meet; drive additionally needed a per-code `Override` — BZ148→413, BZ149→423 — and an `Extend` hook — BZ149's HolderID hint). (A shared `QueryJSON` briefly lived here too; the W4 native-RLS leg deleted it — chat's `queryChatJSON`/meet's `queryMeetJSON` must GUC-scope via `dbscope.RunUserScoped`, so a pool-taking helper here would be a scope-bypass footgun.) The per-package `bzSlug` table STAYS local to each consumer package (`ProblemOptions.Slugs`) — only the projection mechanics (Classify/Detail wiring, status/type/code assembly, the optional Override/Extend escape hatches) live here. Each consumer keeps a thin same-named wrapper (`writeChatProblem`/`writeMeetProblem`/`writeDriveProblem`) so the dozens of existing call sites across each package are untouched. `wire_test.go` is the BZ→status→problem table test (known code, unrecognized code, Override, Extend) — chat's/meet's/drive's own `TestBZStatusMap` (in `chat/handler_test.go`/`meet/handler_test.go`/`drive/problem_test.go`) still pin each package's own `writeXProblem` wrapper + `bzSlug` table unchanged. **Protocol-reply projection** (`projection.go`): `Classify(err) → ReplyClass` with `HTTPStatus()`/`SMTPReply()`/`JMAPError()`/`WebDAVStatus()`/`LogLevel()` — one table mapping a BZ code to each wire's reply, consumed by `jmap.mapReducerErr`, `smtp.classifyReducerReply`, and `carddav`/`caldav` (which previously returned a blanket HTTP 500 for every reducer error). **Single-source + machine-guarded (arch A1):** classification lives in ONE `codeClass map[string]ReplyClass` (client-actionable codes → a 4xx class) plus an `intentional500` set (internal invariants that deliberately 500) — collapsing the former two-source split (`validErrorCodes` + a separate `Classify` switch) that recognized only 13 of ~90 codes. `Classify` is now a map lookup (HINT-disambiguating the dual-use `BZ001`/`BZ081`); `CodeIsKnown` is membership across both. Added `ClassConflict` (HTTP 409) for state-transition/clash codes. `guard_test.go`'s `TestEveryRaisedBZCodeIsClassified` globs `sql/reducers/` + `sql/dbschema/` and **build-fails on any raised `USING ERRCODE='BZ…'` absent from both maps** — so the ~58 codes that used to silently `serverFail`/500 are all classified now and can't regress. `LogLevel()` is the single source of truth for the Info-vs-Error split: a client-actionable class (forbidden/not-found/over-quota/stale-state) logs at `Info` (expected, recoverable), `ClassUnknown`/`ClassTransient` (the 500/serverFail fallback) at `Error`. The `caldav`/`carddav` `writeReducerError` and `scim`'s `logReducerError` route their one log line through it instead of hard-coding `Error`; `jmap.mapReducerErr` keeps the equivalent split inline. **c6 — reducererr is the CONSUMED error contract:** `detail.go` adds `Detail(err) string`, the single wire-detail projection (per-code passthrough allowlist — `passMessage` for the jmap-validation/access-review/protection/JIT/approval/offline-device families, `passHint` for the CA family incl. BZ102 — then per-code fixed strings, then a per-class fallback; an unlisted code NEVER leaks raw SQLERRM/HINT). `ClassUnprocessable` (HTTP 422) added for semantically-bad-content codes. The former hand-rolled handler switches were reconciled INTO the classification, keeping each handler's live wire status: BZ043→invalidArguments/400 (quicksteps), BZ064→409 (admin_protection), BZ085→400 (caErrorStatus), BZ088/090/092→422 + BZ091→404 (ca_import), BZ095→409 (device_lockdown), BZ099 slug-split not_found→404 / last_global→409 (admin_offline), BZ100→409, BZ104/105→400 (admin_jit), BZ106/108→409 + BZ107→403 + BZ109→422 + BZ110→409 (approval). `contract_test.go` is the GOLDEN WIRE MATRIX (one row per code × slug: status/JMAP type/detail policy; a coverage test forces a row for every classified code) and pins the SPA hard contracts (`cannotCalculateChanges`; CA 409/400 for state-helpers.ts). `IsCode(err, codes...)` is the sanctioned seam for SEMANTIC branches only (BZ101 idempotent wipe-ack, BZ015 app-password-cap 409 body, BZ046/47 fixed body, BZ052 mdm, approval email-page renders, calendar BZ001); wire projections must use Classify/Detail. `scripts/check-reducererr-contract.sh` (self-testing, in run-guards.sh) bans BZ-literal `pgErr.Code` branches outside `shared/reducererr/` (allowlisted: `user/undo_send.go` — bespoke BZ021→404/BZ022→410 map; `mailctl/ca_verbs.go` — HTTP client; authserver's `terms.go`/`auth.go` are no longer allowlisted as of arch #A8 — they now route through `reducererr.Classify`/`Detail`/`IsCode` like every other consumer). The SYNTHETIC 403+BZ095 JSON body in `middleware/device_trust_gate.go` (the SPA deviceBlock contract) is not a pgErr path and is pinned by `TestDeviceLockdownGate_SyntheticBZ095Body`. |
| `restore` | Disaster recovery: mode detection, signed-manifest/config-digest verification, reducer-classified safe config apply with unknown/encrypted/endpoint/trust settings quarantined, blob restore, single-flight lifecycle and integrity report. Driven synchronously only through the filesystem-permissioned local Unix control socket; the public router mounts no restore mutation. |
| `updater` | U1–U4 root-owned transactional execution substrate. Mirrors the V178/V179 state graph; persists a versioned SHA-256/fsync journal with torn-write/checksum/order rejection; fences one local and one DB-session executor; binds an intent to an owner-pinned immutable release manifest and the exact fresh recovery-set UUID/digest; and resumes exact response-loss boundaries. U2 adds strict artifact staging, immutable release switching, expand migrations, fixed systemd/readiness, automatic rollback, and fixed-path recovery. Retained hosts require the `VerifiedRecoveryPointProvider`; only explicit disposable fixtures may bypass it. The installed daemon conditionally loads the fixed host step catalog only when root-owned execution config, database execution gate, and verified recovery evidence all allow execution. Ubuntu success/rollback gates pass; rollout defaults stay OFF. `updater/testutil` roots are TestOnly and rejected by production trust. |
| `recoveryset` | Mailserver-internal compatibility aliases over `shared/recoveryset` plus tests. The portable contract verifies HMAC/freshness/installation/timeline, every component and referenced blob, protected-key evidence, feature-toggle digest, and new-target restore proof. The manifest object is the sole commit point and is always written last. |
| `scim` | SCIM 2.0 server (RFC 7643/7644): Users + Groups CRUD, PATCH, filter, deletion policy. Disable/delete (`disableUserByID`/`DeleteUser`) fire `RevokeAuthSessions` (exported) — a POST to the authserver's `/auth/scim/disable` keyed on `user_id` (not `scim_external_id`, which a locally-created user lacks — the prior external-id-only call silently skipped revocation for that case). This is the ONE seam mailserver uses to reach that endpoint; `admin.UsersHandler`'s suspend branch (`admin_users.go`) reuses the same function for its own best-effort immediate revocation rather than rolling its own HTTP client. The structural guarantee is the disabled-user check in `authserver/internal/token/refresh.go` (refresh is refused for `users.enabled=FALSE`); this call is only for immediacy. |
| `smime` | S/MIME signing, encryption, verification, decryption; CMS/PKCS#7; X.509 cert management. `VerifyDetached` (via `github.com/github/smimesign/ietf-cms`) is the inbound badge-path verifier; its `VerifyResult.LeafPEM` field (set ONLY on a fully-`valid` verdict — empty otherwise) is the SP1 Phase-2 harvest input, so an untrusted/invalid signer is never harvestable. `LoadBundledRoots` supplies the embedded Mozilla CA bundle (Phase B adds admin-imported roots). `Verify` is the legacy iMIP path verifier. `ResolveAndSign`/`IsSignedMessage` (`sign_outbound.go`) are the Phase-D outbound-sign helpers used by `EmailSubmission/set`: From↔SAN cert resolution (`CertMatchesEmail`/`CertEmails` in `verify_detached.go`) + signing, plus the already-signed sniff. `Sign()` (`smime.go`) emits RFC 5652-conformant CMS with 5 interop fixes landed in D4 (FullBytes embedding for cert/issuer/attribute values, correct IssuerAndSerialNumber layout, RFC 5652 §5.4 SET encoding for the signed-attrs digest); verified by the openssl canonicalization oracle (`sign_canonical_test.go`). **`Sign()` output is NOT round-trippable through `VerifyDetached`/`ietf-cms`** (see `doc.go`) — outbound only, openssl/external-MUA verified. Reason markers for sign failures live in `sign_outbound.go`: `ReasonNoCert`/`ReasonNoMatchingCert`/`ReasonSignFailed`. **SP1 recipient-encryption spine** (`resolve.go`, `canonemail.go`): `ResolveRecipientCert(ctx, q, senderUserID, recipientEmail) (*x509.Certificate, bool)` is the single cert-resolution entry the submission check and encrypt step share — it unions internal `user_smime_certs` (by canonicalized email) with sender-owned `recipient_smime_certs` and returns the freshest-by-`last_seen` survivor; fail-closed (`ok=false`) on none / DB error / empty recipient. `IsEncryptionCapable(cert)` is the one shared predicate (RSA ∧ (keyEncipherment OR no keyUsage ext)), mirroring `Encrypt`'s RSA-only rule so a resolved cert can't 500 at encrypt. `CanonRecipientEmail(ctx, q, raw)` layers alias→primary resolution (via the `aliases` table) atop `identity.CanonicalizeAddress`, fail-soft to the identity-canonical form. `Querier` is the package's minimal `QueryRow`-only DB seam (both `*pgxpool.Pool` and `pgx.Tx` satisfy it). New table `recipient_smime_certs` (per-user, `owner_user_id`-scoped recipient cert store): `sql/dbschema/V025__recipient_smime_certs.sql` + pgTAP `sql/tests/recipient_smime_certs_schema.pg`. **SP1 Phase-2 harvest** writes this store via the `smime_harvest_recipient_cert(p_owner_user_id, p_email, p_cert_pem, p_fingerprint, p_not_before, p_not_after)` UPSERT reducer (`sql/reducers/smime_harvest_recipient_cert.sql` + pgTAP `sql/tests/smime_harvest_recipient_cert.pg`): lower-cases+trims email, fixes `source='harvested'`, ON CONFLICT(owner_user_id,email,fingerprint) bumps `last_seen` (preserves `first_seen`), returns the row id. Called best-effort from the inbound `deliver()` harvest hook (`smtp/smime_harvest.go`) on a valid verdict. **SP1 Phase-5 multi-key decrypt** (`decrypt_multi.go`): `DecryptMulti(ciphertextDER, []DecryptKey) ([]byte, error)` tries EACH of the reader's keystore keypairs against the EnvelopedData's RecipientInfos (key rollover + encrypt-to-self) and returns the cleartext from the first serial-match that fully decrypts; `ErrNoMatchingKey` otherwise (the `wrong_key` read-state) — DISTINCT only from a parse error returned before the match loop. **Bleichenbacher-hardened:** the RSA session-key unwrap uses `rsa.DecryptPKCS1v15SessionKey` with a per-attempt random 32-byte (`aesSessionKeyLen`) fallback — NOT `rsa.DecryptPKCS1v15`, whose returned error is a PKCS#1 v1.5 padding oracle — and a matched-but-failing unwrap/body is DELIBERATELY collapsed into `ErrNoMatchingKey` so the caller cannot distinguish invalid padding from a wrong key (`TestDecryptMulti_NoPaddingOracle`). Single-key `Decrypt` got the identical fix; both share the extracted `decryptBody`. `DecodeEnvelopedDER(artifact)` normalizes either the `application/pkcs7-mime` MIME/base64 wire form OR bare DER (e.g. `openssl cms -encrypt -outform DER`) into EnvelopedData DER. Verified by the **reverse openssl oracle** (`TestReverseOracle_OpensslEncryptThenOurDecrypt` in `encrypt_canonical_test.go`): `openssl cms -encrypt` → our `DecryptMulti`. |
| `smtp` | Inbound MTA (port 25), submission MSA (port 587), outbound worker; SPF/DKIM/DMARC/ARC/greylisting/ClamAV/antispam/DANE/MTA-STS/TLS-RPT/sandbox/milter/link-protect/anti-phishing/DSN/journaling/vacation (checks run inline in `deliver()`; the never-wired "configurable pipeline" was removed) |
| `spam` | Per-user ML spam model: FTRL-Proximal online learner, feature extraction, model store, trainer; admin dashboard |
| `startup` | SeaweedFS/S3 bootstrap; partition provisioning; startup recovery report; secrets auto-generation; WORM initialization |
| `storage` | Storage drivers (S3, OSS, POSIX, backup-S3) behind the `Driver` interface; blob storage migration; WORM (Write-Once Read-Many) enforcement; retention. `Driver` is now the **factory + object-I/O** interface (return type of `NewDriver`, consumed by `startup/worm.go` + `admin_setup.go`/`admin_storage_migrate.go`), **not** a consumer interface — see the consumer-defined interfaces note below. **WORM/immutability is a separate `Retention` interface** (split out of `Driver`): a backend that supports Object Lock also implements `Retention`; `RetentionOf(driver) (Retention, bool)` detects the capability so `startup/worm.go` fails loudly when WORM is configured on a non-capable backend, instead of an `ErrRetentionNotSupported` leaking out of a later object-lock call |
| `telemetry` | Privacy-first device telemetry handler + store (`POST /telemetry`) |
| `testutil` | Shared test helpers: Docker-based integration setup plus the side-effect-free route recorder/atomic owner-artifact writer used by private registrar tests |
| `tlsutil` | TLS config builder; certificate monitor; connection limiter |
| `updates` | Air-gap update feed: upload bundle → serve appcast + assets locally |
| `user` | User-facing REST API: rules, signatures, sender lists, view prefs, IMAP settings, undo-send, recall, external sender banner, notification preferences, quarantine self-service, newsletter unsubscribe, org chart, devices (`devices.go` — list/rename/revoke + **`WipeAck` `POST /offline/wipe-ack`** (offline #2b): authenticated user endpoint called by the native client after it has wiped the offline-core `'mail'` store and destroyed its SQLCipher key; calls `offline_device_wipe_ack(mailboxID, deviceID)` → sets `user_devices.wipe_acked_at`; BZ101 on owner mismatch), login history, active sessions, security score, webhooks, offline size-estimate (`offline_size.go` — `GET /offline/size-estimate`, offline #1b; cross-mailbox-for-one-user aggregate on `workerPool`/BYPASSRLS with an explicit R1 `mb.user_id` filter, allowlisted `BYPASS`; no migration), mailbox quota self-view (`quota.go` — `GET /user/quota`; `mailboxes`-only used/quota sum scoped by R1 `user_id`, on the regular app `pool`), **sharing & delegation self-service** (`sharing.go` — `SharingHandler{pool, flags, logger}`, spec 2026-06-27-sharing-delegation-design.md §6: `Overview` `GET /me/sharing` (who-can-see panel: delegates + explicit calendar grantees + own user-scope `sharing_defaults` rows + inherited org/OU/group rows + `defaultsEnabled` toggle flag), `GrantDelegate` `POST /me/sharing/delegates` / `RevokeDelegate` `DELETE /me/sharing/delegates/{userId}` (→ `mailbox_delegate_grant`/`_revoke` reducers on the caller's primary mailbox, R2; BZ114→404, BZ115→400 via `reducerErrorStatus`+`reducererr.Detail`), `SetDefault` `PUT /me/sharing/default` / `ClearDefault` `DELETE /me/sharing/default/{id}` (→ `sharing_default_set`/`_clear` with `owner_scope='user'` forced server-side; `audienceRef` defaults to the caller's domain for `everyone`); app pool, own rows only; tests `sharing_test.go`) |
| `webdav` | WebDAV protocol base (used by CalDAV and CardDAV). `ResolveProperties[V]` is the one ordered requested/allprop authority: a typed lookup produces deterministic present/missing descriptors, typed nil is missing, explicit empty requests stay empty, and `BuildPropstat` fixes 200-before-404 grouping/status strings. CalDAV/CardDAV retain only their typed XML request catalogs and value installers; unavailable allprop values are omitted while explicitly requested unavailable values render empty elements in the 404 propstat. Also owns shared XML types + `WriteMultistatus`/`MatchETag`, and the RFC 6578 sync-collection helpers: `SyncTokenPrefix`/`BuildSyncToken`/`ParseSyncToken` (opaque token codec), `SyncChanges` (the `caldav_changes` reducer result shape), `WriteValidSyncTokenError` (403 `DAV:valid-sync-token` precondition), and `Response.Status` (top-level 404 for a removed sync member). |
| `webhook` | Outbound webhook dispatcher with circuit-breaker, HMAC signing and delivery log. R4 adds `PublicationWorker` on the typed `pgxutil.WorkerPool`: unconditional startup/residue drain, bounded 100-fact claims, fixed Dispatcher-wide eight-worker executor with bounded admission, current-scope SQL fan-out, stable CloudEvent IDs and at-least-once HTTP. `StartRetryWorker(lifecycleCtx)` is the single executor lifecycle owner and runs before handlers; submit-before-start fails closed. Durable HTTP work is admitted only after `webhook_delivery_claim` atomically leases at most the executor's free slots with `SKIP LOCKED`; settlement is fenced by `(delivery_id,lease_owner)`, including circuit-open retry state, so saturation cannot starve behind a hot prefix. Generic subjectless dispatch matches only explicit `organization`; persistent compatibility events use typed `DispatchForUser` or `DispatchForMailbox`, with mailbox ownership proven in SQL. Saturated legacy best-effort events are explicitly dropped+logged. |
| `wellknown` | `/.well-known/setjoin` — platform discovery document |
| `ws` | WebSocket push handler: upgrades `/jmap/ws`, fans out `pg_notify` events to connected clients. **Control-payload pass-through (offline #2b):** `classifyBatch` (`ws/handler.go`) returns a 5th value `controlPayloads []json.RawMessage` — messages whose `"type"` field is in `controlPayloadTypes` (`"offline.wipe"`, `"capsChanged"`). These are forwarded to connected WebSocket clients even when no JMAP state changed, and **even to revoked-device connections** (so the device receives its `offline.wipe` command before the revocation gate can drop it). **Push-token acceptance (arch-hardening C1):** the WS handshake (`ws/handler.go`) and SSE handshake (`ws/eventsource.go`) both validate their out-of-band token (cookie/query param, no `Authorization` header to route through `chain.AuthenticateRequest`) via the single `auth.Chain.AuthenticatePushToken(tokenStr)` seam (`internal/auth/chain.go`) — replacing two inline `jwt.Validate` call sites that had each hand-rolled the same two guards. It rejects a non-access token (`ErrNonAccessTokenOnPush` — a refresh token has empty `Scp`, so this is what actually stops it) and a scoped/mirror token (`ErrScopedTokenOnPush` — non-empty `Scp`, e.g. the Drive native-token bridge, is a REST-surface credential and must never open a full JMAP push session); both handshakes collapse either rejection into one 401, the distinction not observable to clients. Pinned by `internal/auth/chain_test.go` + `scripts/check-scope-containment.sh`. **Chat presence (Task 10):** both handshakes, around their existing `chat:{uid}` subscribe block, call `chat.Presence.Connect(claims.UserID)` / deferred `Disconnect` — the in-memory connect-count registry `GET /chat/presence` reads (owned by `internal/chat`, imported here; no DB, no push). |
| `xinchuang` | Xinchuang (国产) DB adapter interface (`-tags xinchuang`): domestic DB drivers behind common interface; polling-based LISTEN fallback |
| `secevents` | Security-event outbox drain (L2-C4): `security_event_outbox` (V253, FORCE RLS deny-all, owner setjoin_owner) + worker-only `security_event_enqueue` (function ACLs + pinned search_path per the provision-roles:201 carve-out; atomic in-tx `app_notify`). Worker claims `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` with `attempts < max` cap, claim-generation-fenced settlements, per-tick bounded drain batch + guaranteed exhausted-claim terminalization, 90s Deliver deadline < 120s lease, at-least-once with stable event IDs. Cold-wired with a nil sink (inert) until a production sink is admitted; escalation via per-check consecutive-streak health checks (`security_event_outbox_failed` 0 / `security_event_outbox_pending_age_minutes` 15/60; 20min/3-checks defaults). LISTEN (`-tags xinchuang` notifier) is a latency optimization only — the outbox is the delivery guarantee. `internal/secevents/worker.go` |

**Identity connector security boundaries.** AD and LDAP accept only `ldaps` or
verified `starttls`; bind credentials are never sent before TLS. Their live
enabled resolver is checked every cycle, and disable, invalid configuration, or
reload failure cancels and removes the worker instead of retaining stale
directory authority. Connector-neutral `identitysource.ApplyPage` validates and
materializes an omitted batch UUID in its own frame before staging, so batch
begin, entry application, and cursor commit always use one exact durable ID;
direct `Begin` callers retain the same defensive validation. Manager
reconciliation occurs only after a terminal page,
resolves the page's manager DN to the same source's immutable subject, and
clears stale source-owned `manager_id` when the fact is absent or unresolved.
Normalized SCIM User list/filter/direct/delta operations are scoped to the
authenticated token's exact active source link; delta tokens are source-bound,
and sparse PATCH sends only fields actually present so an unrelated patch
cannot re-enable or clear concurrently managed state. Identity job follow-up
and diagnostic artifact routes require unscoped identity-admin authority before
their first database or storage access.

Organization-global identity inventory, SSO group mappings, source detail,
diagnostics, jobs, and upgrade controls authorize from
`claims.GlobalPermissions`, the live resolver subset backed only by assignments
with no domain, OU, or scope-set narrowing. Effective scoped permissions alone
never grant those routes. `IdentityHandler` holds its worker database behind the
minimal `Query`/`QueryRow` interface while production still injects the worker
pool; this keeps malformed/null/error reducer responses and permission
rechecks independently testable without weakening the database-owned authority
boundary.

SCIM Group list/get/create/PATCH/delete and membership resolution are likewise
bound to the authenticated token's exact active source. V168 stores
`distribution_groups.scim_source_id`, keys external IDs by source, and removes
the old source-agnostic reducer overloads. Membership mutation resolves only
active provisioning links from that same source, excludes guests, rejects an
unresolved or foreign external ID atomically, and preserves manual/SSO-owned
rows. User PATCH/DELETE first performs this uniform source-bound lookup so a
guest or foreign identifier is the same 404; `PUT active:false` commits the
disable and then calls the shared immediate session-revocation seam.
V169 removes the interim generation-less Group reducer signatures. Create,
display-name replace, delete, membership replace/add, and membership remove all
pass the token-bound source generation and lock/recheck it inside the same
transaction as their mutation. Removal resolves the durable same-source
provisioning subject even after its link is suspended, so deprovisioning cannot
leave a hidden source-owned membership; addition and replacement still require
an active link.

Member LiveKit admission is reducer-serialized through
`identity_media_mint_fence`. It records a short-lived admission lease under the
same per-person advisory lock used by transition execution/rollback. Member
tokens are capped at 60 seconds at the Go mint seam; a transition snapshots
every live admission and its eviction horizon, and the unconditional effect
worker repeats removal until that horizon. Authenticated lobby polling calls
the same fence, so polling cannot mint around a pending transition.

V171 extends the unconditional `identitytransition` worker with
`identity_session_eviction_intents`. Identity unlink snapshots exact
room/`u:<user_id>` targets in the unlink transaction after revoking OAuth,
web-session, WebSocket, and link-elevation state. The worker claims with
`SKIP LOCKED` and repeats success, not-found, and transient removals through the
bounded stale-token horizon, so a participant connecting after the first remove
is still evicted. Forward and rollback subscriber-notice effects emit their
transition ID and state through explicitly typed PostgreSQL text parameters, so
the generic JSON builder cannot fail parameter inference and strand the durable
effect.

**`drive` Guest G2 chunk-HEAD extension.** The version-manifest possession arm
in `HEAD /drive/chunks/{sha256}` also passes the referenced version through
`chatAttachmentLiveSQL`: a chat-bound version proves possession only while its
attachment is above the caller's current immutable history floor. Non-chat
spaces retain the normal manifest proof, and the caller's own upload-claim arm
remains independent.

**`ws` Guest G2 EventSource extension.** An authenticated guest claim is
mailbox-less by contract, so `/jmap/eventsource` does not resolve a primary
mailbox, subscribe to mailbox StateChange channels, or emit an initial JMAP
StateChange. Session-revoke remains independent of app access. Each self-scoped
Chat/Drive/Meet subscription is guest-only gated through
`check_user_app_entitlement` before subscribe or presence side effects: a
Chat-denied guest gets neither `chat:{user_id}` frames nor a presence connect,
and Drive/Meet are suppressed analogously. Non-guest sessions retain both the
primary-mailbox requirement/403 failure and their pre-G2 subscription topology.
The DB-backed
`TestGuestEventSourceChatFrame` proves a member commit arrives as an actual
`event: chat` frame carrying the committed message id.

**`pushjobs` Guest G1 extension.** `DrainGuestDigests` is the one exported
Run/tracer seam. It drains `guest_notification_intents` after chat on every
tick regardless of `feature.guest_accounts`: 15-minute default throttle,
recent-session skip, 50-row batch, and a per-guest
`pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` across status→render→mail→stamp (safe behind the
production transaction-mode PgBouncer pool). That transaction deliberately
stays open across the MinIO write under a 30-second timeout so the
`outbound_queue` insert and exact intent stamp commit atomically; any
store/queue/send failure rolls both database writes back. The same transaction
owns every status/render read. Rendering is guest-scoped for
mentions/DMs/meetings; frozen/missing/deleted refs are tombstoned, and a 7-day
sent/stale reaper bounds residue. Disabled/expired guests are tombstoned
without mail, active sessions leave intents pending, and the digest subject
count is derived only from authorization-approved rendered lines (never raw
claimed rows). The DB-backed `worker_test.go` matrix pins
no-spin skips, cap/stamp, queue+stamp transaction identity, send rollback,
two-worker exactly-once, no session-lock topology, cross-guest privacy, and
both reaper arms.

### Drive node ACL and managed-cache revocation

V184–V193 extend `internal/drive` without changing its role: Go parses bounded
wire input and runs user-scoped reducer calls; it never recomputes effective
access. `acl_governance.go` owns exact access projection, bounded subject search,
grant/inheritance preview and mutation, quick views, and governed access-request
routes. `access_sync.go` owns the opaque invalidation page and epoch-consistent
visibility snapshot. Every ordinary Drive read/list/mutation route now delegates
to the canonical reducer capability instead of a copied membership predicate.

Chunk existence/upload and version finalization require a complete ACL binding
(user, node, space, epoch, upload authorization); partial bindings fail before a
storage or DB oracle. Access revocation appends targeted invalidation evidence;
web/Electron clients consume it before content events, run a full snapshot on an
unknown cursor, and quarantine/delete bytes no longer visible. Chat/Meet binding,
Guest profile, classification, export, WORM, and retention remain outer caps.
The four behavior flags are seeded OFF and maintenance drains stay unconditional.
Tests live in `acl_governance_test.go`, `access_sync_test.go`, chunk/content live
tests, the web sync matrix, and the Electron fake-server/mirror/service suites.
See ARCH-020 and `docs/guides/DRIVE_ACL_OPERATIONS.md`.
Receipt-backed grant/request creation rejects a missing, duplicated, malformed,
or body-mismatched `Idempotency-Key`. Lost-response grant and revoke replays are
HTTP-pinned; revoke replay never substitutes for current `manage_acl` authority.

### Guest G4 Drive tier contract

Authenticated guests reach only the exact Drive attachment surfaces enumerated
by `guestgate`; G4 adds `GET /drive/versions/{id}/preview` and deliberately does
not add space discovery, mutation, export, audit, search, lock, or native-token
routes. `content.go` calls `drive_content_download_require` before resolving a
version, so raw-byte denial is constant-shaped BZ215 and anti-oracle. `preview.go`
uses the same Chat attachment liveness/history-floor predicate as content.
`memberscope.go` caps the Chat-derived role at viewer for guests while leaving
meeting attendance membership independent of download policy. The JMAP guest
runtime policy exposes fail-closed `allowDownload` only as a presentation hint;
SQL remains authoritative and `apps.drive` stays false because there is no
standalone guest Drive app. `chunks.go` calls `drive_chunk_upload_require`
before staging; `CommitStagedChunk` repeats it in the final transaction, locks
the space classification plus the canonical `(scope, sha256)` key, and only
then promotes and registers. The helper denies every guest account regardless
of its derived role (including a meeting host whose locked attendance mapping
remains manager). This ordering is load-bearing because an allowlisted guest
PUT must fail BZ140 without writing an object that can never receive a claim.

### Drive canonical object lifecycle

`chunks.go` is the single storage protocol for HTTP uploads and Meet recording
ingest: hash/size verification writes only `tmp/upload/`; the final short DB
transaction rechecks upload authority with a `FOR SHARE` space lock, acquires a
PgBouncer-safe `pg_advisory_xact_lock` derived from `(scope, sha256)`, promotes
with `CopyObject`, and calls `drive_chunk_register` before commit. Registration
also takes the same advisory lock and removes any stale zero-ref GC queue row;
the seven-day claim sweep requeues an upload that is never referenced.

`worker.go` uses that same key lock for both zero-ref GC and canonical orphan
inventory. GC commits the authoritative DB-row deletion before storage removal,
then opens a second locked transaction for the final row recheck plus
`RemoveObject`; a failed DB commit can therefore never restore a row whose bytes
were deleted. Inventory rotates a process-local cursor across at most 200
`drive/` objects per tick, accepts only `drive/g/<sha256>` or
`drive/s<positive-id>/<sha256>`, requires age greater than 24 hours, and deletes
only while a locked `drive_chunks` absence check remains true. Unknown age/key,
DB/list/storage errors, fresh objects, registered deduplicated objects, and
WORM-locked objects all fail safe and remain for a later pass.

## Features / Capabilities

### SMTP — Inbound MTA (port 25) — `smtp/inbound.go`, `smtp/pipeline*.go`

- **SPF check** — RFC 7208 `MAIL FROM` domain SPF validation; `feature.spf` toggle; calls `spf_check()` reducer (`smtp/spf.go`)
- **DKIM verification** — RFC 6376 signature verification on inbound; `feature.dkim` toggle (`smtp/dkim.go`)
- **DMARC evaluation** — RFC 7489 policy enforcement with p=/sp= handling; `feature.dmarc` toggle; DMARC aggregate reports stored for TXT-record discovery (`smtp/dmarc.go`, `smtp/dmarc_report.go`)
- **ARC (Authenticated Received Chain)** — RFC 8617 seal verification for forwarded mail; `feature.arc` toggle (`smtp/arc.go`)
- **Greylisting** — Triplet-based defer with `greylist_check()` reducer; auto-prune via cron; `feature.greylisting` toggle
- **ClamAV antivirus** — Streaming scan via ClamAV daemon; configurable address; `feature.clamav` toggle. Commands use clamd's newline-framed `nINSTREAM`/`nPING`/`nVERSION` form; bare legacy commands fail against modern ClamAV with `UNKNOWN COMMAND`. A reused connection permits exactly one complete exchange at a time, and any deadline/write/read/protocol failure closes and invalidates it before reuse (`smtp/clamav.go`).
- **RBL/DNSBL** — DNS-based blocklist lookup; `feature.rbl` toggle; RBL results fed to antispam scorer
- **Spam scoring** — `feature.spam_scoring` toggle; per-user ML model via `spam/` package; `feature.ml_spam` toggle (`smtp/antispam.go`). **`shared/spam` detector suite (arch review #A5)** — `smtp/ml_detectors.go`'s `applyMLDetectorSignal` folds `DetectSenderMismatch`/`DetectForgedRecipients`/`DetectMixedCharset`/`DetectFakeHTMLScore` into `AntiSpamResult` as ADDITIVE signal (`AntiSpamResult.DetectorScore`, never replacing the FTRL score), gated `toggle.FeatureSpamMLDetectors` (`feature.spam_ml_detectors`, DEFAULT OFF, V137) — off, `deliver()`'s scoring path is byte-for-byte what it was before this wiring. `shared/spam`'s other detectors (archive/content_verify attachment-byte checks, htmlstruct phishing-template match, imagehash, spamtrap, the tokenizer+multiclass second classifier, the composites YAML rule engine) stay unwired pending infra (attachment-byte extraction, a template/hash feed, a trained model store, a rule config file) — see `mailserver/internal/smtp/ml_detectors.go`'s doc comment for the per-detector rationale. Enabling in production (threshold tuning, false-positive validation on real mail) is a separate follow-up, ledgered in TODOS.md.
- **Attachment sandbox** — Pluggable `SandboxAnalyzer` interface; `AttachmentAnalysisPipeline` with configurable timeout and fail mode (`smtp/sandbox.go`)
- **Milter integration** — External milter daemon (RFC 6647 protocol via `d--j/go-milter`); soft-fail if milter is down
- **Anti-phishing** — Three sub-detectors: display-name spoofing, contact-graph lookalike, Unicode confusable domains (`smtp/antiphish.go`, `antiphish_contact.go`, `antiphish_display.go`, `antiphish_lookalike.go`, `smtp/confusables.go`)
- **STARTTLS** — Both inbound (opportunistic) and submission (enforced); `SetTLS`/`SetTLSEnforce` per server (`smtp/tls_policy.go`, `tlsutil/tlsutil.go`)
- **Per-IP rate limiting** — Configurable `perIPRateLimit` on inbound connections (`smtp/inbound.go`)
- **Max connection limit** — `SetMaxConns` + per-user semaphore (`tlsutil/limit.go`)
- **S/MIME inbound verification** — Gated by `feature.smime_verify` (`toggle.FeatureSmimeVerify`), which **ships ON** (V013 seeds the row, V032 flips it `enabled=true` as the cutover default; the toggle-registry default `false` stays the conservative no-row safety net). Fail-open — a verification error never blocks delivery. Runs on the PRISTINE message bytes BEFORE link-protect/anti-phish body rewriting; a fully-`valid` verdict (chain to the bundled Mozilla roots via `smime.LoadBundledRoots()`, Email-Protection EKU, valid validity window, and From-match) bypasses those body mutators and is stored. The graded verdict (`smime_status` + signer fields) is persisted by `deliver()` and emitted by `jmap_email_get`. Phase B will add admin-imported private-CA roots.
- **S/MIME signer-cert harvest (SP1 Phase 2)** — On a fully-`valid` inbound verdict, `deliver()` calls `c.harvestSignerCert` (`smtp/smime_harvest.go`) to UPSERT the verified signer's leaf cert (carried on `VerifyResult.LeafPEM`, set only on `valid`) into `recipient_smime_certs` for the RECEIVING user (`owner_user_id` resolved from the receiving mailbox's `user_id`, never `req.*`; `source='harvested'`; fingerprint = sha256 of the DER via `smime.Fingerprint`) through the `smime_harvest_recipient_cert` reducer (ON CONFLICT bumps `last_seen`, preserves `first_seen`). **Valid-only** (`untrusted`/`invalid`/`unsigned` never harvest) and **best-effort** — any failure (unresolved recipient, shared/no-owner mailbox, fingerprint or upsert error) logs at Warn/Debug and returns; harvest NEVER blocks or fails delivery. Cross-tenant carve-out on the worker pool (see the native-RLS note); `recipient_smime_certs` is not a `messages`/`message_tags` table, so `check-rls-coverage.sh` does not gate it.
- **Delivery pipeline** — Calls `deliver()` PL/pgSQL reducer per recipient; vacation auto-reply (`feature.vacation`), rules engine (`feature.rules_engine`), journaling (`feature.journaling`), retention hooks (`feature.retention`), audit log (`feature.mailbox_audit_log`), external-sender first-time banner (`feature.external_sender_first_time`) all gated by feature toggles passed as reducer session variables (`smtp/pipeline_deliver_store.go`)
- **Reducer-error → SMTP reply classification (`smtp/reducer_error.go`)** — `handleData` no longer collapses EVERY `deliver()` failure to a generic transient `451`. `classifyReducerReply(err)` decodes the BZ code (`reducererr`) and picks PERMANENT (5xx — the sending MTA must NOT retry) vs TRANSIENT (451 — may retry) per `docs/reducer-error-codes.md`: **BZ001 over_quota / BZ009 domain_over_quota / BZ004 sending_quota → `552 5.2.2`** (mailbox full), **BZ002 forbidden / BZ003 send_as_unauthorized / BZ005 dlp_blocked → `550 5.7.1`** (policy), **BZ007 no_mailbox → `550 5.1.1`** (unknown recipient), **BZ008 missing_inbox → `451 4.3.0`** (transient infra/config). A non-BZ infra error returns `ok=false` and the existing safe transient `451` stands (a blip is retried, not bounced). The reply line is a fixed generic per-class string — the reducer's human message is never leaked. This stops a sending MTA from retrying a permanent failure forever.
- **Link protection (inbound)** — URL rewriting in message body for click-time scanning (`smtp/link_protect.go`)
- **DSN (Delivery Status Notifications)** — Per RFC 3464; generated for permanent failures (`smtp/dsn.go`)
- **DLP patterns** — Data Loss Prevention pattern matching on inbound content (configured via admin API, evaluated in reducer)
- **DMARC live monitor / failure feed** — Stored in DB; queryable via `GET /admin/dmarc/live`, `/failures`, `/trend` (`admin/admin_dmarc.go`). The failure feed reads the canonical per-message SPF/DKIM/DMARC verdicts from `messages`, orders by `received_at`, and scopes domain administrators through the receiving mailbox's `domain_id`; do not revive the retired `message_trace.traced_at`/`dmarc_fail` shape or expose cross-domain forensic rows.

### SMTP — Submission MSA (port 587) — `smtp/submission.go`

- **AUTH PLAIN + LOGIN** — SASL mechanisms; credential verification via `bcrypt` (local) or forwarded to auth chain; threat interceptor integration
- **STARTTLS required** — Enforced before auth; configurable via `SetTLSEnforce`
- **Threat interceptor integration** — Pre-auth threat pipeline (rate limit, tarpit, lockout) via `shared/threat` (`smtp/submission.go`)
- **Per-user connection limit** — `SetPerUserMaxConns`; per-user concurrency semaphore
- **Tag-on-send (B3)** — `onSuccessUpdateEmail` patches applied post-submit via `jmap_email_submission_set` reducer; tags message with Sent/user-specified labels on successful delivery (`mailserver/sql/reducers/jmap_email_submission.sql`, `jmap/handler.go:emailSubmissionSet`)
- **Native-RLS (W4)** — the submission server is constructed on the `workerPool` (BYPASSRLS), NOT the tenant app pool: `send()` does local-recipient delivery (`deliver()` into other mailboxes — cross-tenant). Submission self-authenticates at SMTP AUTH and `send()` self-authorizes the sender (send-as/delegation) before any write, so no tenant GUC is needed. INERT until W6. See the DB connection model section.
- **Reducer-error → SMTP reply classification** — the `send()` failure path routes through the same `classifyReducerReply` (`smtp/reducer_error.go`) the inbound MTA uses: a permanent reducer failure (BZ004 sending_quota `552 5.2.2`, BZ002/BZ003/BZ005 policy `550 5.7.1`, BZ007 `550 5.1.1`) returns the precise 5xx so the submitting client surfaces a hard error instead of the old blanket `554`; a non-BZ infra error preserves the existing generic `554 5.3.0 Failed to send message`.

### SMTP — Outbound Worker — `smtp/outbound.go`

- **MX resolution + delivery** — DNS MX lookup; EHLO, MAIL FROM, RCPT TO, DATA sequence; connection pool per MX host. Every SMTP session has bounded command/body I/O deadlines and a context-cancellation closer, so a silent MX cannot strand `outbound_queue.status='sending'` or delay process shutdown indefinitely. Successful sessions remain open for reuse and start the next transaction with `RSET`; never send `QUIT` and then place that dead session in the pool. Every `textproto.Cmd` response is paired with `StartResponse`/`EndResponse`, including multiline EHLO and DATA's final reply, so pooled-session sequencing cannot deadlock.
- **DANE TLSA** — RFC 7671/7672 TLSA lookup and certificate authentication; `DANESecure`/`DANEInsecure`/`DANELookupFailed` states (`smtp/dane.go`)
- **MTA-STS** — RFC 8461 policy enforcement for outbound TLS (`smtp/mtasts.go`)
- **TLS-RPT** — RFC 8460 reporting worker; builds and submits daily TLS session reports; `_smtp._tls` TXT discovery (`smtp/tlsrpt.go`)
- **Link-protect egress un-rewrite (A2)** — a forwarded copy (`outbound_queue.forward_unwrap_links=TRUE`, set by `apply_action`'s `forward_to` branch) carries the inbound message's stored body whose external URLs were rewritten to THIS server's `/link-protect?token=…` redirects (broken for the forward target; leak the recipient mailbox_id + message_id in the token). The worker un-rewrites them back to the original URLs (`linkprotect.UnwrapMessageBody`, verifies each token's signature first, leaving foreign/tampered tokens untouched) **before** DKIM so the signature covers the clean body. Secret = `cfg.JWTSecret` via `OutboundWorker.SetLinkProtectSecret` (mirrors the inbound rewriter).
- **DKIM signing** — RFC 6376; signs outbound with domain key from DB (`smtp/dkim.go`, `outbound.go:signDKIM`)
- **S/MIME signing (Phase D — opt-in per submission, signed at submission)** — CMS/PKCS#7 detached signature; signs with the user's own cert from DB. **Phase D re-homed signing from the SMTP worker into the `EmailSubmission/set` Go handler** (`jmap/mail_smime.go`): on submit, the handler resolves the signing cert by **From↔cert-SAN match among the user's OWN certs** (R1, keyed on `user_id`; `is_default` only breaks a tie — NOT the legacy `users.email=From` join), fetches the draft blob, signs it via `smime.ResolveAndSign` (which mirrors the worker's exact header/body split + `smime.SignMessage` byte-handling so external MUAs still accept the signature), stores the signed blob (content-addressed `sha256` key, the `messages.raw_ref` convention), and feeds the new blob key per creationId into the **`p_processed_raw_ref` swap seam** on `jmap_email_submission_set` (4th arg, renamed from `p_signed_raw_ref` in V027; the reducer swaps `raw_ref` in-txn and orphans the old blob). Sign failures become per-creationId `notCreated[*].{type,description}` entries built Go-side and merged into the reducer's `SetResponse` — markers: `smime_no_cert`, `smime_no_matching_cert`, `smime_sign_failed`. An already-signed draft (sniffed by `smime.IsSignedMessage`) is not re-signed. The handler strips `signSMIME` from the create map before the reducer call. The outbound worker signing path, the `sign_smime` column, and the `v_sign_smime`/`BZ075` reducer guard were removed by V011 (Phase D3). The `app.encryption_key` GUC needed by `pgp_sym_decrypt` is carried by `h.pool`'s `AfterConnect` hook (same db pool builder as the worker). User self-service cert management is via `/auth/me/smime/certs` (list/upload/delete + `{id}/default` set-default); the first uploaded cert auto-defaults.
- **S/MIME encryption (SP1 Phase 3 — opt-in per submission, encrypted at submission + at rest)** — CMS EnvelopedData. **Re-homed from the outbound worker to the `EmailSubmission/set` Go handler** (`jmap/mail_encrypt.go:processSubmissions` → `encryptOneSubmission`), so it runs ONCE before the local/external split and before content scanning is bypassed. On submit, for each `encryptSMIME` creation the handler: runs the content pipeline (DLP/rules) on the CLEARTEXT inside `send()`/`deliver()` (the reducer is handed the cleartext bodies and stores NULL); resolves the cert set = {every recipient via `smime.ResolveRecipientCert`} ∪ {sender's own cert — **encrypt-to-self**, so the Sent copy is readable} ∪ {journal cert when `journaling_required_for_domain` — **encrypt-to-journal**}; **signs-inner / encrypts-outer** (`smime.SignMessage` then `smime.Encrypt`) when both requested; stores the ciphertext content-addressed; and feeds `jmap_email_submission_set` the `p_processed_raw_ref` swap (V027) + `p_is_encrypted` (V027). **Encrypted-at-rest:** `send()`/`deliver()` null `body_text`/`body_html` for the encrypted copies (V028); the FTS trigger `trg_messages_tsv()` sets `body_tsv = NULL` for a `crypto_type='smime-encrypted'` row (V029 — ciphertext is genuinely never full-text indexed; `subject_tsv`/`from_tsv` kept since those headers stay cleartext); `raw_ref` = the ciphertext; `subject` retained. **FAIL-CLOSED:** ANY missing recipient/sender/journal cert (or encrypt error, or no infra) → per-creation `notCreated` (`smime_encrypt_no_cert` / `smime_encrypt_no_sender_cert` / `smime_encrypt_no_journal_cert`), the creation is DROPPED, nothing sent, NEVER plaintext. The **single shared predicate** `smime.IsEncryptionCapable` (RSA ∧ keyEncipherment-or-no-keyUsage) gates resolution so a resolved cert can't 500 at encrypt. The worker no longer encrypts (the dead `messages.smime_encrypt` gate column was dropped in **V031**; encrypt intent is per-submission `encryptSMIME`, never a stored column — guarded by `smtp/smime_encrypt_gate_test.go` + a `hasnt_column` pgTAP assertion). **Remaining limit:** a mixed recipient set where any recipient lacks a usable cert fails the whole creation closed (no partial-plaintext fan-out).
- **S/MIME decrypt-on-read (SP1 Phase 5 — read side, webmail/JMAP only)** — `Email/get` does HOSTED decrypt-on-read for `cryptoType='smime-encrypted'` rows (`jmap/mail_decrypt.go:decryptEmailResult`, wired into `emailGet` after the reducer): load the READER's keystore keypairs (`loadDecryptKeys`, R1-scoped, `pgp_sym_decrypt(private_key_enc::bytea, …)` under the `app.encryption_key` GUC), fetch the `raw_ref` EnvelopedData blob, `smime.DecryptMulti`-try ALL keys (rollover + encrypt-to-self), inject **transient** `bodyText`/`preview` + a `decryptState` (`decrypted`/`no_key`/`wrong_key`/`error`). **Plaintext is NEVER persisted** (the DB body columns stay NULL after a read); a failed decrypt is a calm read-state, never a 5xx. Each success is audited (`mailbox_audit_log` action `email_decrypt_read`); the pass is **cost-bounded** per user (`decryptAllowed`, 120/min — a denied op serves the encrypted read-state, never plaintext, spec C5). The webmail read states (D-F1) live in `MailRow.svelte` (Lock placeholder + `inbox.encrypted_placeholder`, never a blank snippet) and `ReadingPane.svelte` (the D-F4 Lock "Encrypted" chip — distinct from the verified ShieldCheck, shown alongside it when signed+encrypted — and the two calm can't-decrypt panels for `no_key`/`wrong_key`/`error`, never red, with a Settings → Security link). **IMAP NEVER decrypts** (C3): it serves the ciphertext blob verbatim so the client's own key opens it. The bug fix landed alongside: `loadSignCandidates` had the same uncast `pgp_sym_decrypt(private_key_enc, …)` — `pgp_sym_decrypt` has no `(text,text)` overload, so signing was failing at runtime; both sites now cast `::bytea`.
- **Scheduled send (B2)** — `message_schedule()` enqueues a `send` `scheduled_action` (A6); the `dispatch-scheduled-actions` cron + `scheduled_action_send` handler move Scheduled→Sent at `fire_at`; `EmailSubmission/cancel` cancels the pending action before dispatch
- **Undo-send** — `drain_outbound_hot()` cron (every 2s) delays immediate sends by user-configured interval; `POST /messages/{id}/undo` revokes pending submission
- **Outbound queue** — Retry worker (`drain_outbound_queue(true)` every 1m); force-retry/bounce via admin API (`admin/admin_queue.go`)
- **Delivery-attempts side-log (Phase 2a)** — the worker writes one `delivery_attempts` row per recipient per attempt (best-effort, error-ignored, OUTSIDE the delivery txn; skipped when `message_pk`/`mailbox_id`/`received_at` is absent — legacy/DMARC sends). `sendEnvelope`/`sendData`/`deliverToMX` now surface the remote SMTP `(code, text, tls)` (the `250`/`354` gate decisions are byte-for-byte unchanged; `parseSMTPCode` is panic-free). The per-recipient outcome loop (`deliverRecipients`, behind the `loadRawFn` test seam) also fixed two latent multi-recipient `message_trace` bugs: the 'delivered'/'bounced' rows now name the CORRECT recipient (not `recipients[0]`) and build `details` via `json.Marshal` (not `fmt.Sprintf`). **`message_trace` stays MESSAGE-LEVEL** — one 'delivered' per delivered message (`writeDeliveredTrace`, correct recipient + `{delivered,total}`), one 'bounced' at the queue-bounce moment only (`markFailed`'s `status='bounced'` branch) — so `admin_reports` sent/bounced COUNTs are not inflated; per-recipient/per-attempt detail lives ONLY in `delivery_attempts`.
- **Per-domain TLS policy** — Configurable `require_tls`/`starttls` policy per domain; outbound TLS enforcement (`smtp/tls_policy.go`)

### SMTP — Journaling — `smtp/journal.go`

- **Compliance journaling** — `drain_journal_queue()` cron dispatches copies of messages to configured journal address; `feature.journaling` toggle

### IMAP4rev2 Bridge — `imap/imap.go`, `imap/imap_limits.go`

- **LOGIN** — Auth PLAIN with bcrypt verification; threat interceptor integration for brute-force protection; per-user TOTP enforcement (rejects if TOTP enabled, requiring Bearer flow instead)
- **CAPABILITY** — Advertises `IMAP4rev2 LOGIN-REFERRALS ID ENABLE` per RFC 9051, plus `STARTTLS LOGINDISABLED` before a configured TLS upgrade; plaintext `LOGIN`/`AUTHENTICATE` are refused with `PRIVACYREQUIRED`
- **SELECT / EXAMINE** — Opens a mailbox by IMAP path; reads user IMAP settings for visible tag set; returns FLAGS, EXISTS, UNSEEN, UIDVALIDITY, UIDNEXT
- **LIST** — Enumerates user-visible mailboxes with per-user IMAP visibility settings; maps tag hierarchy to IMAP folder paths
- **STATUS** — Per-mailbox status: MESSAGES, UNSEEN, UIDNEXT, UIDVALIDITY
- **FETCH** — UID FETCH and sequence-number FETCH; supports BODY[], BODY[TEXT], BODY[HEADER], RFC822, FLAGS, ENVELOPE, INTERNALDATE, UID; blob-backed message retrieval from SeaweedFS
- **STORE** — Flag mutations (`\Seen`, `\Flagged`, `\Deleted`, `\Draft`, `\Answered`) mapped to JMAP label state via reducers
- **SEARCH** — Server-side message search via reducer; supports common IMAP SEARCH criteria
- **APPEND** — Upload raw RFC 2822 message to a mailbox; calls deliver reducer
- **EXPUNGE** — Permanent deletion of `\Deleted`-flagged messages
- **UID** — All commands also available in UID form (UID FETCH, UID STORE, UID SEARCH, UID EXPUNGE)
- **IDLE** — RFC 2177 IDLE command; pushes EXISTS/EXPUNGE notifications from `pg_notify` via LISTEN dispatcher
- **STARTTLS** — In-session TLS upgrade on 143 using the shared certificate; implicit TLS is enabled on 993 by default when that certificate exists
- **Per-user connection limit** — `SetPerUserMaxConns`; drops excess connections
- **Webhook dispatch** — Certain IMAP events (flag changes, new messages) trigger outbound webhooks via `webhook.Dispatcher`
- **Fuzz testing** — `fuzz_test.go` with corpus in `testdata/`
- **Native-RLS (W5 wrap + W6 FORCE flip applied; LATENT until the prod DSN switch)** — every `messages`/`message_tags`-touching command runs inside a **per-op** scoped transaction via the `connState.withScope(ctx, fn)` helper (fork 3: per-op wrap, NOT a pinned connection — pinning binds a Postgres backend per long-lived IMAP session and dies at scale). `withScope` does `BEGIN → SELECT set_config('app.current_mailbox_id', c.mailboxID::text, true) → fn(tx) → COMMIT` (mirrors `jmap` `poolCaller.CallScoped`); the GUC is transaction-scoped so it auto-clears at commit — no `AfterRelease` reset and no separate IMAP pool (that isolation-only optimization is deferred). The GUC is keyed on **`mailbox_id`** (ARCH-003 currency, set in `handleSelect`), never the folder — an IMAP "folder" is a **tag** (an app predicate, `c.tagID`), per CONTEXT.md "A folder is a tag; `messages.mailbox_id` is immutable". **Wrapped** (touch `messages`/`message_tags`): `handleFetch`, `handleSearch`, `handleStore`, `handleExpunge`, `handleMove`, `handleCopy`, `handleClose`, `handleAppend`, the `handleSelect` next-UID fallback, and the `idleWithNotify` EXISTS counts. **Left unwrapped** (W6-safe — touch only `tags`/`mailboxes`/`folders`/`user_imap_settings`/auth/audit tables, never `messages`/`message_tags`): `handleLogin`, `handleAuthenticate`, `handleStartTLS`, `handleList`, `handleStatus`, `handleCreate`, `handleDelete`, `handleRename`, `handleUnselect` (the dispatching `handleUID` is not itself wrapped — it delegates to the already-wrapped FETCH/SEARCH/STORE/COPY/MOVE; UID COPY/MOVE now route to the real `handleCopy`/`handleMove` instead of replying a fake `OK …`), and the `auditLog` `mailbox_audit_log` insert. Streaming commands (FETCH/SEARCH/STORE/EXPUNGE) drain + close their `pgx.Rows` **inside** the tx before any write and before Commit (never held across Commit), reading into an in-memory slice and formatting/streaming to the client after the scope returns (EXPUNGE streams its untagged responses from inside `fn`). **W6 proof: `imap_isolation_test.go`** drives the REAL handlers as the constrained `setjoin_app` role under V004's FORCE'd tables (a buffer-backed `net.Conn` + a `connState` bound to tenant A; fixtures inserted via a separate superuser pool) and asserts cross-tenant isolation end-to-end — A sees only A's rows, a STORE/EXPUNGE scoped to A cannot touch B; non-vacuity by an RLS-off control (the desync FETCH leaks A's row without RLS). DB-gated on `DB_DSN` (skips without a DB). See `retired-docs:superpowers/plans/archive/2026-06-18-native-rls-backstop.md` (W5/W6). **P1 IMAP fix (folder-is-a-tag, T1–T7 — landed):** APPEND/MOVE/COPY/CLOSE used to resolve messages via the legacy `folders` table — a join of `messages.folder_id` against `folders.id`, or `folder_id = (SELECT id FROM folders WHERE name=…)`. But `messages.folder_id` holds a **TAG** id (the `trg_message_tags_primary_sync` trigger sets `folder_id = NEW.tag_id`), and `folders`/`tags` are independent id sequences, so those verbs silently no-op / orphaned messages. Now all four resolve via the `message_tags`+`tagID` model (the FETCH/SEARCH/EXPUNGE pattern: `JOIN message_tags mt … WHERE mt.tag_id = c.tagID AND mt.is_primary = TRUE`). Per verb: **APPEND** now (a) resolves the dest folder as a TAG by name+mailbox from `tags`, (b) supplies the three NOT NULL columns the old INSERT omitted (`message_id`/`thread_id`/`raw_ref` — its INSERT failed outright before), (c) gets the per-tag UID via `next_imap_uid_for_tag`, and (d) creates the **primary** `message_tags` row via `tag_message(…, is_primary=TRUE)` so the message is visible (was silent data loss). **MOVE** batch-loads the source view (kills the per-seq N+1), captures each ORIGINAL `received_at`, removes the source tag membership, then adds the dest — a location-group dest routes through `transition_message_location()` (exclusive invariant + state bump + notify), a user-tag dest through `tag_message(…, TRUE)`. **COPY** adds the dest tag (`is_primary=TRUE`, `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`) at the original `received_at` WITHOUT removing source (duplicate). **CLOSE** uses the EXPUNGE `message_tags` pattern. The `idleWithNotify` EXISTS counts (the IDLE new-message push) had the same `folder_id = (SELECT id FROM folders …)` bug — always counted 0, so IDLE never fired — now on the `message_tags`+`c.tagID` pattern. **T7**: the dead `UPDATE folders SET total_count …` writes in EXPUNGE/CLOSE/APPEND were removed — `folders.total_count` has no reader (tag counters are maintained by `trg_message_tags_counters` on `tags`), and that write previously errored + rolled back the whole tx. Reducer-vs-raw: MOVE/COPY/APPEND prefer the `transition_message_location`/`tag_message` reducers; COPY's add-without-remove + idempotent re-copy uses a raw `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` (no single reducer cleanly expresses it). Functional proof: **`imap_verbs_functional_test.go`** drives the real handlers via the `imap_isolation_test.go` harness (APPEND-then-FETCH, MOVE gone-from-source/present-in-dest + location-invariant, COPY duplicates, CLOSE expunges) — DB-gated, self-cleaning.

### JMAP Core + Mail — `jmap/handler.go`, `jmap/blob.go`

Vendor capability URN: `urn:setjoin:params:jmap:mail`

**Delegated sender identities:** each enabled delegated account in the Session
payload carries raw `delegateGrants` plus `sendAsAliases` in its per-account
vendor capability. `sendAsAliases` is ordered and populated only for
`send_as`/`full_access`; read-only and send-on-behalf-only accounts receive an
empty list. Compose must submit a selected shared alias with that delegated
`accountId`. `send.sql` remains authoritative and its mailbox-ownership check
is NULL-safe, because a shared mailbox intentionally has `user_id NULL`.
`send_on_behalf` is deliberately distinct: it may submit through the shared
account only with the delegate's own canonical address or an alias on the
delegate's enabled primary mailbox; it cannot impersonate the shared primary
address or any shared alias.

**Architecture invariant — reducer calls go through the `reducerCaller` seam (`jmap/caller.go`).** A reducer-shaped call (`SELECT fn($1,…)` scanning one JSON column) is issued as `h.caller.Call(ctx, "fn", args…)`, not as a hand-written `h.pool.QueryRow`. The 50 pure-positional reducer-call sites across `handler.go` / `calendar_a3.go` / `contact_group.go` / `reminder.go` / `quicksteps.go` are routed; `Call` builds the `$1,…` placeholder list from `len(args)`, so the emitted SQL, arg order, error mapping and `serverFail`/`"database error"` fallback are byte-for-byte unchanged. This makes the translate→reduce→error-map middle (arg shaping, the limit clamp, the BZ→JMAP map) unit-testable with no Postgres via `fakeCaller` (`caller_test.go`). **What is NOT routed** (stays on `h.pool` directly, same scope boundary as the ~20 raw-SQL/`Exec`/multi-row sites): reducer calls whose SQL carries a per-placeholder cast (`$2::JSONB`, `$3::timestamptz`), a literal arg (`…, NULL)`), a dynamically-chosen function name (`"SELECT "+sqlFn+"(…)"`), or a non-JSON / multi-column scan — `Call`'s fixed placeholder rendering cannot reproduce those byte-for-byte. The handler keeps the `pool` field for those sites; `parseAccountID` continues to route account resolution through `shared/identity`.

**Reducer error → JMAP type map is one shared helper: `mapReducerErr` (`jmap/reducer_error.go`).** Every reducer-call failure routes through `mapReducerErr(err, h.logger, method)` instead of the old ~49 identical inline blocks that collapsed EVERY reducer error to `serverFail`. It decodes the stable BZ code (via `reducererr` — the leaf vocabulary package) and maps ONLY the client-actionable codes from `docs/reducer-error-codes.md` (a client can branch on them); genuinely-internal codes stay on the `serverFail` default so no reducer internals leak. **BZ011 → `cannotCalculateChanges`** (RFC 8620 §5.2 — `jmap_email_changes` raising it on a too-old `sinceState` reaches the client as the resync signal it can act on, not an opaque 500), **BZ006 → `accountNotFound`**, **BZ002 / BZ003 (send_as_unauthorized) / BZ005 (dlp_blocked) → `forbidden`**, **BZ001 over_quota (mail) / BZ004 (sending_quota_exceeded) / BZ009 (domain_over_quota) → `overQuota`**, **BZ007 (no_mailbox) → `notFound`**, everything else → `serverFail`/`"database error"` (the default arm preserves prior behavior byte-for-byte). **BZ001 is dual-use** (over_quota in mail, forbidden/notFound in calendar — the catalog note); the generic map routes it on the HINT.type slug (`over_quota` → `overQuota`, all other BZ001 slugs fall through to `serverFail`) so a calendar BZ001 reaching this default is not mis-labeled `overQuota`. Recognized business errors log at `Info` (expected, client-recoverable); the `serverFail` default logs at `Error`. The detail string comes from `reducererr.Detail` (c6) — the per-code fixed wording the former `jmapDetail` switch and the domain-specific helpers held (BZ038 “reminder not found”, BZ039, BZ042, BZ045, the forbidden/overQuota family lines) plus the BZ040/BZ041/BZ043 message-passthrough allowlist — so the former per-domain helpers (`reminder.go reducerError`, `quicksteps.go quickStepError`, `email_templates.go`/`savedsearches.go` wrappers, the `contact_group.go` BZ045 branch) are DELETED; their call sites use `mapReducerErr` directly. The one survivor is `calendar_a3.go calendarA3Error`: the dual-use calendar BZ001 meaning is call-site-contextual (deliberately `ClassUnknown` in the shared table), so it branches via `reducererr.IsCode(err, "BZ001")` → `forbidden`. Out of scope (deliberately not routed): the `quicksteps.go` tx-machinery errors (Begin/set_config/Commit), `blob.go` HTTP `WriteProblem` paths, and the `identity.go` raw-`users` "user not found" lookup — none are reducer business errors.

**Core (RFC 8620)**
- `Core/echo` — round-trip test
- `/.well-known/jmap` — Session endpoint with capability declaration
- `POST /jmap/upload/{accountId}` — Blob upload to SeaweedFS
- `GET /jmap/download/{accountId}/{blobId}/{name}` — Blob download from SeaweedFS. `blobDownloadAuthorized` admits the own-mailbox owner (unchanged) OR (#13 shared-mailbox use-surface, the Download delegate arm a33c315c deferred) a caller holding ANY mail delegate grant (`read`/`send_as`/`send_on_behalf`/`full_access` — mirrors Upload's `gateRead` check) on `accountId`, when the blob is attached to a message IN that mailbox; the grant lookup rides `identity.ValidateAccountID` (per-request/uncached — fail-closed on revocation) and the ownership `EXISTS` runs on `workerPool` (same BYPASSRLS shape as `blobGet`). Proof: `blob_download_test.go` (`_DB` suite).
- `GET /jmap/ws` — WebSocket upgrade for push events; JMAP `Request` frames over WS resolve §3.7 result references via the SAME evaluator the HTTP batch uses (`JMAPHandler.ResolveReferences`)
- `GET /jmap/eventsource` — the JMAP push channel over Server-Sent Events (RFC 8620 §7.3), the WS-fallback for proxies that block WS upgrades (advertised as `eventSourceUrl` in the session object). Same `notify.Dispatcher` subscription + `StateChange` machinery as `/jmap/ws` (`ws.Handler.EventSource`, sharing `buildChangedTokens`/`classifyBatch`), emitting `text/event-stream` frames. Honors `types=` (type filter), `closeafter=state` (one-shot current-state fetch), `ping=N` (keepalive event). Auth: `Authorization: Bearer` (origin check skipped) or the `access_token` cookie (same-origin pinned, like WS).
- Result reference resolution (RFC 8620 §3.7): each `#argName` is a `ResultReference{resultOf,name,path}` evaluated by JSON-Pointer against the named prior response (`jmap/resultref.go`); failed resolution → `invalidResultReference`. Both the HTTP batch and the WebSocket transport resolve them identically.

**Blob (RFC 8620 §6)**
- `Blob/get` — Fetch blob metadata
- `Blob/copy` — Cross-account blob copy
- `Blob/lookup` — Find blob by hash

**Mailbox (RFC 8621 §2)**
- `Mailbox/get` — Fetch mailbox objects
- `Mailbox/changes` — Delta sync by state token; `maxChanges` parsed for forward-compat but NOT forwarded (reducer is still 2-arg; Task 6 deferred); BZ011/BZ006 codes fixed (pre-#3 raised bare strings); returns empty delta when resync sentinel drives full folder resync
- `Mailbox/query` — Filter/sort mailboxes
- `Mailbox/queryChanges` — Paginated change feed
- `Mailbox/set` — Create, update, destroy mailboxes

**Email (RFC 8621 §4)**
- `Email/get` — Fetch email with property projection; blob-backed body retrieval
- `Email/query` — Full filter/sort including mailbox, keyword, thread, date ranges
- `Email/changes` — Delta sync by `jmap_email_state`; `maxChanges` forwarded as `nullIfZero(req.MaxChanges)` (nil → SQL NULL → reducer DEFAULT NULL); soft-paged via `hasMoreChanges`; BZ011 on pruned/out-of-range sinceState; BZ006 on unknown mailbox
- `Email/set` — Create, update (keyword/flag changes), destroy; snooze extension (see below)
- `Email/deletedQuery` — Vendor extension: query soft-deleted messages
- `Email/recover` — Vendor extension: undelete messages
- `Email/purge` — Vendor extension: hard-delete messages

**Email — snooze extension (B1, `urn:setjoin:params:jmap:mail`)**
- `Email/set` with `snoozeUntil` property — routes to `message_snooze()` reducer (applies the Snoozed tag + enqueues a `snooze` `scheduled_action`); the `dispatch-scheduled-actions` cron + `scheduled_action_snooze` handler wake them; `snoozeUntilOnEmailSet: true` declared in Session capabilities

**Email — newsletter bundles (B5.2, `urn:setjoin:params:jmap:mail`)**
- `Email/bundles` — Vendor method: computed newsletter bundle list grouped by `messages.list_id`. Request: `{accountId, limit?, position?}`. Response: `{accountId, bundles:[{listId, count, latestEmailId, latestReceivedAt}], position}`. Excludes Trash/Spam and opted-out list_ids (per-user opt-out: `users.view_prefs->'newsletterOptouts'` JSON array). Backed by `newsletter_bundles_list(p_account_id, p_limit, p_offset)` reducer. Gated by `feature.newsletter_bundles` toggle (surfaced in `effectiveCapabilities.features.bundles`). Vendor flag `newsletterBundles: true` declared in Session capabilities.

**Thread (RFC 8621 §3)**
- `Thread/get` — Fetch thread objects
- `Thread/changes` — Delta sync by thread state

**SearchSnippet (RFC 8621 §7)**
- `SearchSnippet/get` — Highlighted search snippets for email query results

**Identity (RFC 8620 §4)**
- `Identity/get`, `Identity/changes`, `Identity/set` — Sender identities (display name, from aliases)

**EmailSubmission (RFC 8621 §7)**
- `EmailSubmission/set` — Submit message; creates outbound queue entry; supports `sendAt` for scheduled delivery (B2). **S/MIME (sign + encrypt at submission):** before the reducer call the handler runs `processSubmissions` (`jmap/mail_smime.go` sign + `jmap/mail_encrypt.go` encrypt) — sign-inner/encrypt-outer, encrypt-to-self + encrypt-to-journal, store the final blob, feed `p_processed_raw_ref` + `p_is_encrypted` (the 4th/5th reducer args) — and strips `signSMIME`/`encryptSMIME` from the create map; the outbound worker no longer signs OR encrypts (V011/D3 + SP1 Phase 3). FAIL-CLOSED: any missing recipient/sender/journal cert drops the creation into `notCreated`, never plaintext. Encrypted copies are stored encrypted-at-rest (bodies NULL, `raw_ref` = ciphertext; V028). **Native-RLS (W4):** this is the one **cross-tenant** JMAP method (`send()`→`deliver()` writes into other mailboxes) — it routes through `h.workerCaller.Call` (bypass pool, no tenant GUC), correcting the W3 `CallScoped` mis-classification that would deny the local-recipient delivery under FORCE.
- `EmailSubmission/cancel` — Cancel a pending undo-send or scheduled submission (sender-scoped → stays on `CallScoped`)
- `EmailSubmission/get` — List pending/deferred submissions (Scheduled-tagged messages); `emailSubmissionGetAndQuery: true` in capabilities
- `EmailSubmission/query` — Query by `sendAt`, ordered; `sendAtOnEmailSubmissionSet: true` in capabilities

**Tag-on-send (B3, via RFC 8621 §7.5)**
- `EmailSubmission/set` with `onSuccessUpdateEmail` — applies label patches (e.g. remove Drafts, add Sent) after successful submission via `jmap_email_submission_set` reducer

**VacationResponse — via MailTips and inbound reducer**
- Vacation auto-reply status queried in `HandleMailTips`; enabled/disabled via `vacation_settings` table; `feature.vacation` toggle controls reducer invocation

**Read receipts (MDN, RFC 3798) — Track A privacy**
- `POST /auth/me/read-receipt` — `HandleReadReceipt` (`jmap/handler.go`) relays the recipient's MDN decision. Body `{messageId, consent}`; `consent` (default false) is forwarded as the 4th arg of `send_read_receipt($1,$2,$3,$4)`. The reducer is the hard privacy gate: the per-user `read_receipt_policy` decides — `never` suppresses regardless of consent, `ask` needs `consent=true`, `always` sends. The handler never decides; it only relays.
- Inbound MDN auto-processing (sender-visible read rollup) is DEFERRED — see `docs/specs/MDN_DEFERRED.md`.

**MailTips**
- `GET /auth/me/mailtips` — Returns context-sensitive compose warnings: external domain, large recipient list, vacation status of recipients, DLP-sensitive domain indicators (`jmap/mailtips.go:HandleMailTips`). External-recipient warnings require both the default-on organization `feature.external_sender_warn` and the authenticated user's default-on `users.external_sender_warning`; missing flags/preference lookups fail closed for that advisory while OOO and other MailTips continue independently.
- `POST /auth/me/dlp-check` — DLP compose-time policy tip (Purview-style): dry-runs the draft against the `dlp_evaluate_draft` reducer, the SAME `dlp_check` seam `send()` enforces at send-time (`jmap/mailtips.go:HandleDlpCheck`). Warn/quarantine/notify_admin render as an advisory tip; only `block` disables Send client-side (send-time BZ005 remains the hard gate either way).

**Effective capabilities (B8.3, `urn:setjoin:params:jmap:mail`)**
- Session object includes a top-level `effectiveCapabilities` key resolved from the `effective_capabilities(p_account_id)` reducer, with app flags for mail/calendar/contacts/tasks/chat/drive/meet plus non-app feature flags including `external_labels`. A successful member lookup preserves absent-row default-allow; guests default-deny absent rows. DB/nil errors fail closed for every app flag and `external_labels` while retaining the conservative non-app defaults.
- Vendor capability flag `effectiveAccess: true` signals to clients that the server computes per-user entitlements and enforcement middleware (B8.4) is active.

**Admin console visibility (M7, S6 layer 1, `urn:setjoin:params:jmap:mail`)**
