- Vendor capability sub-key `admin: {consoles: [...], permissions: [...]}` (built by `adminCapabilityBlock` in `jmap/handler.go`) exposes two things. **`consoles`** — the admin console ids the user may access, resolved via `admin.ConsolesForPerms(user.Permissions)`; the five possible values are `"mail"`, `"system"`, `"logs"`, `"auth"`, `"access"` in declared order; `"access"` (the Role-group editor console) is gated on `roles.manage`; a non-admin receives an empty array. **`permissions`** — the caller's OWN resolved permissions, which the SPA reads (`auth.ts` `adminPermissions` store + `hasPermission`) to gate each write control on its exact permission, so a read-tier admin gets disabled controls instead of buttons that 403. Exposing the caller's own perms is self-knowledge the server enforces regardless (and is trivially probable by clicking) — not a disclosure boundary; the prior "no raw perms" minimalism was reversed by the 2026-06-22 decision. It is ONLY the caller's own resolved set — never the full permission catalog or another user's perms.
- Clients read this once on session load and render console navigation (rail icons, sidebar, drawer entries) from `consoles`, and use `permissions` to disable write controls the caller lacks. **Server-side enforcement (layer 2, `RequirePermAudited` middleware) is the authority; the client gate is UX only** (it prevents the enabled-button-that-403s defect). Client gating is wired across **all** admin write controls (~130 controls over ~27 pages + the AppAccessEditor / IPRulesDialog / SodEnablementPanel child dialogs), each gated on the exact permission its route's `RequirePermAudited` requires — `disabled={!hasPermission($adminPermissions, '<perm>')}`. The map of route→perm lives in `cmd/mailserverd/main.go` (the `require*` wrappers). **Deliberately NOT gated:** console-access-only routes (`RequireAnyPermAudited` over a console's scopes — e.g. S/MIME trust-anchor writes, EAP preview/match-count, rules-test dry-runs: being on the page already implies the access) and read/list/export controls. **Known gap (separate, pre-existing):** a few SPA pages (smtp-pipeline, limits, auth runtime-settings, system backup-config/run, mam rekey) call endpoints NOT registered in the gateway route table — frontend↔backend route drift, left ungated because there is no permission-gated route to mirror; tracked for investigation. The U4 `updates/server` routes are now registered, OpenAPI-covered, and contract-gated. Adding an admin write control on a NEW permission? Add a `CapabilityCap` to `mailserver/webmail/src/lib/admin/capabilityCatalog.ts` — `capabilityCatalog.test.ts` fails until the Access Preview catalog covers it.

**B8.4 — per-app entitlement enforcement**
- `middleware.RequireApp(checker, logger, app)` middleware enforces entitlement at the HTTP layer. `PoolEntitlementChecker` calls `check_user_app_entitlement` directly, so mailbox-less guests use the same gate. CalDAV/CardDAV retain their existing wrappers; Chat/Drive/Meet use `RequireAppFeatureFirst`, preserving toggle-OFF 404 before entitlement deny/error 403. DB errors fail closed. 403s are logged at `Info` level.
- `jmap/handler.go` `Dispatch` also gates gated JMAP methods: `Calendar/*`/`CalendarEvent/*` → calendar, `ContactCard/*`/`AddressBook/*` → contacts, `Task/*` → tasks. Gating applied before method dispatch; returns JMAP `forbidden` error on deny. Gate skipped when `entitlementGate` field is nil (nil-pool / unit-test mode).
- JMAP capability DB/nil failures set every app flag false while leaving non-app defaults unchanged. Guest capabilities consult the same user-id checker for Chat, Meet, and Drive policy. Meet additionally requires the guest program, global Meet toggle, derived entitlement, and fail-closed profile projection. G4 projects fail-closed `allowDownload` only when guest+Drive toggles and derived entitlement are live; standalone `apps.drive` remains false.

### JMAP Calendar — `jmap/handler.go`

- `Calendar/get`, `Calendar/changes`, `Calendar/set` — Calendar container CRUD
- `CalendarEvent/get`, `CalendarEvent/query`, `CalendarEvent/changes`, `CalendarEvent/set` — Event CRUD. `set` create/update carry `valarm` (reminder alerts — absolute-trigger array, threaded verbatim to the reducer like `color`/`freeBusyStatus`).
- `CalendarEvent/import` (`jmap/calendar_import.go`) — bulk-import an uploaded `.ics` file into ONE owned calendar. Args `{accountId, calendarId, ics, strategy}`. The gateway **bounds the payload (≤1 MiB, ≤1000 events) BEFORE parsing**, parses the iCal with the shared caldav parser (`migration.SplitAndParseICS` — the PURE split+parse; the raw-INSERT `migration.ImportICS`/`insertCalendarEvent` path is deliberately NOT used — it bypasses RLS/state/notify + takes an unchecked calendarId), maps each event to the create-shaped object, and calls the `calendar_import_ics` reducer (calendar-ownership verified before any insert; each event routed through `jmap_calendar_event_set_create`). Ownership comes from `parseAccountID` (the caller's mailbox), never a trusted id. Returns `{imported, skipped, errors}` (+ `parseErrors`). Gated by the calendar capability. iCal STATUS is lower-cased + CHECK-validated; attendees/relative-trigger VALARMs are not imported (deferred).
- `CalendarEvent/freeBusy` — Free/busy query for a list of attendees and time range
- `CalendarEvent/queryFreeSlots` — Find open meeting slots across attendees
- `CalendarEvent/rsvp` — Accept/tentative/decline an iTIP invite; triggers `jmap_calendar_event_rsvp()` reducer and sends iMIP reply. Optional `onBehalfOf` (manager email) switches to the delegate-respond path: the manager's primary mailbox is resolved **server-side** (never from `accountId`), and the authenticated `user.UserID` is passed to the reducer as the delegate to gate on `mailbox_delegates`. The same-mailbox `parseAccountID` guard is never relaxed.
- `CalendarEvent/counter` — **Outbound iTIP COUNTER** (`{accountId, eventId, start, end}`): the authenticated invitee proposes new times to the organizer. Calls `jmap_calendar_event_counter()` (owner-scoped + attendee-gated, **NON-MUTATING** — a counter is a proposal, so the invitee's copy keeps its sequence/jmap_state/etag until the organizer accepts) then serializes the proposal as a COUNTER iMIP via `serializeAndEnqueueCounter` → `buildCounterMIME` — the INVERSION of the invite path: `BuildIMip(MethodCounter, …, fromEmail=attendee)`, so From:=attendee / To:=organizer, `outbound_queue.from_addr`=attendee, envelope rcpt=`[organizer]`, DTSTART/DTEND=the proposed times, UID+SEQUENCE unchanged. Async/best-effort (background goroutine, 5-min timeout, panic recovery — a serialization failure never fails the call). BZ001 (foreign mailbox) / BZ002 (non-attendee) → JMAP `forbidden`. *(The inbound organizer-side "someone proposed a new time → one-click reschedule" is deferred — `ProcessITIP` default-warns on COUNTER today.)*
- **A3 calendar parity (`jmap/calendar_a3.go`)** — `Calendar/getShared` + `CalendarEvent/getShared` (open another user's calendar/events; the **viewer is `user.UserID`**, never a body field; owner passed as email the reducer resolves; `freebusy` grants redacted to Busy blocks), `Calendar/shareGet` + `Calendar/shareSet` (calendar_acls grant/revoke; owner/admin-gated; levels freebusy/read/write/admin), `Calendar/delegateList` (managers the viewer may respond for — `full_access`/`manage_calendar` grants), `CalendarEvent/setOccurrence` (occurrence/following/series recurring edit; scope validated before the reducer), `CalendarEvent/resourceList` (bookable rooms for the RoomFinder), `CalendarEvent/sendUpdate` (bumps SEQUENCE + re-issues the iMIP REQUEST via the shared invite path). All gated under the calendar capability/app. **`CalendarEvent/set` create no longer decides in Go** — the two create side-effects are reducer-owned (the gateway only serializes + enqueues, mirroring `sendUpdate`): invites via `calendar_finalize_create_invites` (the recipient set + organizer, read from the stored rows) and resource booking via `calendar_finalize_create_bookings` (email→room resolution + `calendar_book_resource` fan-out, booker = the caller's users.id via `account_scope` — ADR-003). The gateway's `serializeAndEnqueueInvite` (shared with `sendUpdateInvite`) does only `BuildIMip` → blob → `outbound_queue`; invites stay async/best-effort (background goroutine, 5-min timeout, panic recovery — a serialization failure never fails the create). The booking call is synchronous; its per-event outcomes surface as the unchanged `resourceBookings` object on the create result (so the UI can show "Room declined (conflict)"). BZ001 from the reducers → JMAP `forbidden`.

### JMAP Contacts — `jmap/handler.go`

- `AddressBook/get`, `AddressBook/changes`, `AddressBook/set` — Address book container CRUD
- `ContactCard/get`, `ContactCard/query`, `ContactCard/changes`, `ContactCard/set` — JSContact CRUD
- `ContactCard/findDuplicates` — cluster the caller's contacts by `lower(email address)`, return clusters ≥2 (`contact_find_duplicates` reducer)
- `ContactCard/merge` — merge a duplicate into a keeper (field union + group-membership re-point, atomic; `contact_merge` reducer, ownership in the WHERE → BZ112)
- `ContactCard/galSearch` — Search the Global Address List
- `ContactCard/directoryTree` — Hierarchical department/OU directory tree
- `ContactCard/directoryMembers` — Members of a directory node
- `ContactCard/directorySearch` — Free-text search across all directory nodes
- `ContactGroup/get`, `ContactGroup/query`, `ContactGroup/set` — A4: user-created personal contact groups (distribution lists) CRUD; `jmap/contact_group.go` → `jmap_contact_group_*` reducers
- `ContactGroup/expand` — A4: resolve a group to its member recipients (compose "send to group"); owner-scoped (`expand_contact_group`)

### JMAP Tasks — `jmap/handler.go`

- `Task/get`, `Task/query`, `Task/changes`, `Task/set` — Task CRUD with Kanban/list views
- `TaskComment/get`, `TaskComment/set` (QoL #17, `jmap/tasks.go`) — the task activity feed (comments + system change-log) → `task_activity_list` / `task_comment_create` reducers (`jmap_task_comment.sql`, table `task_activity` V102); mailbox id from auth via `parseAccountID` (never `req.*`), R1 ownership enforced in-reducer; `set` resolves `mentionedEmails` to internal users (best-effort deliver notification) and pushes on the existing `task:{mailbox}` channel; BZ040→invalidArguments (bad body/mention), BZ041→ownership/notFound. The status/assignee/due change-log rows are written inline by `jmap_task_set_update`. Capability `urn:ietf:params:jmap:tasks`.
- `Reminder/get`, `Reminder/dismiss`, `Reminder/snooze` (A6, `jmap/reminder.go`) — the Scheduled-Actions reminder surface → `reminders_get` / `reminder_dismiss`(`_all`) / `reminder_snooze` reducers; account id from auth (never `req.*`); BZ038→notFound, BZ039→invalidArguments. Capability `urn:setjoin:params:jmap:reminders` (advertised in Session).
- `QuickStep/get`, `QuickStep/set`, `QuickStep/apply` (A2, `jmap/quicksteps.go`) — Outlook-style Quick Step macro store + allowlisted apply → `quick_step_list` / `quick_step_set` / `quick_step_apply` reducers; mailbox id from auth via `parseAccountID` (never `req.*`); BZ040/BZ041→invalidArguments, BZ042→notFound, BZ043→forbidden.
- `SavedSearch/list`, `SavedSearch/set`, `SavedSearch/delete` (`jmap/savedsearches.go`) — named per-mailbox saved-search folders (persisted queries a user can re-run) → `saved_search_list` / `saved_search_set` / `saved_search_delete` reducers, all via `caller.CallScoped` (per-request `app.current_mailbox_id` GUC for the V080 RLS backstop); mailbox id from auth via `parseAccountID` (never `req.*`). `set` stores the RAW query verbatim; empty name/query → BZ040→invalidArguments.
- `EmailTemplate/list`, `EmailTemplate/set`, `EmailTemplate/delete` (`jmap/email_templates.go`) — compose templates / Quick Parts (reusable per-mailbox body snippets; wires the pre-existing baseline `email_templates` table, no migration) → `email_template_list` / `email_template_set` / `email_template_delete` reducers, all via `caller.CallScoped`; mailbox id from auth via `parseAccountID` (never `req.*`). `set` is upsert-by-name and stores `body_html` VERBATIM (the webmail sanitizes on insert-into-editor); empty name → BZ040→invalidArguments.

> **Known limitation (codebase-wide):** QuickStep/apply — like every JMAP method in this package — resolves `accountId` via `parseAccountID`, which maps to the authenticated user's **primary** mailbox only. It does not yet operate on delegate/shared mailboxes; a non-primary `accountId` fails closed (`accountNotFound`). Tracked for a future delegate-accountId enhancement.

> **Native-RLS (W4) — CalDAV/CardDAV wrapping DEFERRED.** Both bridges run on the **app pool** and are deliberately left un-wrapped. They touch ONLY the calendar/contact tables (`calendar_events`, `contacts`, `address_books`, …) — **never** `messages`/`message_tags` — so the W6 messages-only FORCE flip does not affect them and nothing breaks. Wrapping DAV in a per-request mailbox-scoped GUC tx (and routing FreeBusy cross-mailbox reads onto the bypass pool) is **forward-proofing deferred until/unless native RLS expands to the calendar/contact tables**. DAV has no `reducerCaller` seam (bare pool, 18+9 raw calls), so that future work first builds the seam + resolves a request-scoped mailbox via `identity.ResolvePrimaryMailbox` (handling shared/delegated resources). See `retired-docs:superpowers/plans/archive/2026-06-18-native-rls-backstop.md` (W4).

### CalDAV Bridge — `caldav/caldav.go`, `caldav/ical.go`, `caldav/freebusy_batch.go`

- **CalDAV (RFC 4791)** — PROPFIND, REPORT (calendar-query, calendar-multiget, free-busy-query), PUT, GET, DELETE on calendar events; requested/allprop selection and 200/404 classification delegate to `webdav.ResolveProperties`, while this package owns only the ordered CalDAV XML catalog and typed value/empty-element installation. `calendar-timezone` and schedule URL names use that installer rather than being silently dropped.
- **Free/busy batch** — `POST /calendar/free-busy` — batch free/busy for multiple attendees in one call (`caldav/freebusy_batch.go`)
- **iCalendar parsing/serialization** — `caldav/ical.go`; RFC 5545 compliance tests (`ical_compliance_test.go`)
- **iTIP event dispatch** — Sends iCalendar METHOD:REQUEST/CANCEL emails to attendees on `CalendarEvent/set` creates/updates (`caldav/itip.go`, `caldav/imip.go`)
- **RSVP handling** — Processes incoming iMIP METHOD:REPLY via `caldav/imip.go`
- **Paging** — WebDAV paged PROPFIND results (`caldav/paging.go`)

### CardDAV Bridge — `carddav/carddav.go`, `carddav/vcard.go`

- **CardDAV (RFC 6352)** — PROPFIND, REPORT (addressbook-query, addressbook-multiget), PUT, GET, DELETE; requested/allprop selection and 200/404 classification delegate to `webdav.ResolveProperties`, while this package owns only the ordered CardDAV XML catalog and typed value/empty-element installation.
- **vCard 4.0 import/export** — RFC 6350 serialization (`carddav/vcard.go`)
- **vCard compliance tests** — `carddav/vcard_test.go`

### LDAP GAL Server — `ldap/server.go`, `ldap/dn.go`

- **Read-only LDAP server** — Binds + searches against the Global Address List; RFC 4511 filter evaluation; DN escaping; paged results (`ldap/paging.go`)
- **Directory tree** — Hierarchical OU/department structure browsable via standard LDAP clients (Outlook, macOS Contacts, Thunderbird)
- **Canonical People authority** — successful Bind stores `BoundPrincipal{UserID}`; People base/subtree/Compare resolves its primary mailbox and queries `directory_visible_users`, including its alias/UPN address set. No primary mailbox and database errors fail closed with an LDAP operation error.

### Admin REST API — `admin/`

**Normalized identity People/conflicts** (`admin_identity_people_conflicts.go`,
V166): `GET /admin/identity/v2/people/{id}` calls the reach-scoped
`identity_person_read` reducer and returns a bounded, fingerprint-only detail
model; missing and out-of-scope are the same 404. `GET
/admin/identity/v2/conflicts/{id}` exposes only redacted evidence, candidate
person facts, allowed decisions, and at most 50 append-only decisions. A
domain-scoped caller cannot inspect an unbound source-wide candidate. `POST
/admin/identity/v2/conflicts/{id}/decision` requires `identity.write`, audited
reason, matching body/header UUID, and exact generation `If-Match`, then calls
the single authoritative `identity_match_decide` reducer. Link accepts only
non-soft evidence for the exact reachable member; protected takeover is refused
for the approved transition rail. Create reads only current sanitized inbox
data. Handlers never return raw external subjects or connector payloads.

**Users** (`admin_users.go`):
- `GET/POST /admin/users` — List, create users (with optional welcome email)
- `GET/PUT/DELETE /admin/users/{id}` — Get, update, delete user
- `DELETE /admin/users/{id}` calls `webhook_user_deleted_publish` in the destructive transaction. Flag ON commits one stable fact and suppresses legacy dispatch; rollback commits neither deletion nor fact. Flag OFF retains post-commit legacy delivery through typed `DispatchForUser`, whose SQL predicate is organization scope or exact self owner (never broad payload-derived matching).
- `POST/PATCH /admin/users` now run provisioning/update plus `webhook_user_changed_publish` in one worker transaction. Durable facts approve only `{user_id}`; flag-OFF compatibility retains the historical created payload (`user_id/email/created_by`) and updated payload (`user_id`) after commit through `DispatchForUser`.
- `PUT /admin/users/{id}/password` — Admin password reset
- Admin password reset follows `admin_authority_fence` → `identity_auth_person_fence` → protection/user row → OAuth/session rows, changes the bcrypt hash, and calls `revoke_user_sessions(...,'all','admin_password_reset',...)` before the same commit. V172 statement-level triggers acquire the authority fence before every assignment/JIT, role, department, scope-set/group membership, target department/domain, primary-mailbox, or Guest-host-domain mutation. The reducer's strict permission+reach query denies zero live assignments. The reset therefore cannot race either a refresh successor or assignment/JIT/topology narrowing. Four deterministic two-connection regressions live in `admin_users_password_reset_security_test.go`.
- `GET/POST/DELETE /admin/users/{id}/aliases` — Email alias management
- `GET/PUT /admin/users/{id}/quota` — Mailbox quota get/set
- `POST /admin/users/import` — CSV bulk user import
- `GET/DELETE /admin/users/{id}/app-passwords` — Admin oversight of app passwords
- `GET /admin/users/{id}/app-access` — List the seven per-user app entitlements (`mail`, `calendar`, `contacts`, `tasks`, `chat`, `drive`, `meet`) in deterministic catalog order; missing member rows default allow, missing guest rows default deny, and explicit rows win. Guest classification is folded inside the same scoped relation using the effective ownership domain `COALESCE(guest_account_host_domain(user_id), users.domain_id)` (the RLS-safe form of `COALESCE(guest_accounts.host_domain_id, users.domain_id)`), so a host-domain-scoped admin sees the guest's real values while an out-of-scope/missing target retains the historical member-shaped response and leaks no guest status (`admin_app_entitlements.go`)
- `PUT /admin/users/{id}/app-access/{app}` — Set one of the same seven member entitlements `{enabled bool}`; calls `set_app_entitlement` (audit-logged, BZ033 if missing, 422 if unknown). A direct edit aimed at a guest is reducer-refused with BZ226 and projected through `reducererr.WriteClassified` as 409 with no mutation; guest access remains profile-derived (`admin_app_entitlements.go`)
- `GET /admin/users/{id}/sign-ins` — Per-user login_events drill-down (newest-first, capped 200 rows); sets `app.current_user_id` GUC via `dbscope.RunUserScoped` for RLS (`admin_signin.go`)
- `GET /admin/users/{id}/sessions` — Per-user active oauth families + web sessions (same UNION as self-service `GET /user/sessions`; no current-session flag) (`admin_signin.go`). **Limitation:** the `sessions` table is never populated in the current auth model (auth is JWT + oauth_tokens; the JWT JTI is NOT `sessions.id`), so the web-session half of the UNION always returns empty — the UX is effectively oauth-token-only in production. Future: real web-session tracking is a separate backlog item.
- `POST /admin/users/{id}/secure-account` — Admin force-secure: revokes target's oauth tokens + sessions + calls `secure_account(target, admin, ip, 'admin_secure')` in one tx; audit row attributes the admin actor; requires `users.password_reset` perm (`admin_signin.go`)
- **Domain-scope tenancy (IDOR backstop):** every handler reads `middleware.DomainScopeFromContext` and, when scope > 0, folds the domain into the SAME statement (R1 for reads, R3 for mutations — no separate validate-then-mutate). An out-of-scope id is a no-op → 404 (indistinguishable from missing); Create rejects (403) when the new user's resolved domain ≠ scope; `ImportCSV` rejects each row whose resolved domain ≠ scope per-row (same 403 reason, partial-success preserved); Delete verifies the target's `domain_id` inside the wipe tx before fanning out. scope == 0 = global admin (no predicate). Mirrors `admin_mailboxes.go`. The **app-passwords** (`admin_app_passwords.go` `ListForUser`/`RevokeForUser`), **app-entitlements** (`admin_app_entitlements.go` `List`/`Set`), and **sign-in/sessions/secure-account** (`admin_signin.go`) handlers carry the SAME backstop. App-entitlements folds its effective member/guest ownership domain into every scoped classification/read/write statement; the other handlers use their established `domainGate`/scoped predicates. Regression-guarded by `admin_users_scope_test.go`, `admin_app_access_scope_test.go`, `admin_signin_test.go` (DB-backed, two-domain seed).
- **Guest single-writer protection (Guest Accounts G0 Task 10, C-4):** `adminRefuseGuestTarget(ctx, pool, logger, w, r, id)` guards Update/Delete/ResetPassword (a package-level helper, also applied to `admin_mailboxes.go`'s Create) — 409 problem+json ("manage via /admin/guests") when the target resolves guest, fail-closed (deny) on a lookup error. ResetPassword invokes it only after domain/OU reach and the reducer repeats Guest refusal after the locked current-reach decision, so an out-of-scope Guest has the same 404 as an unknown/out-of-scope member. Reads are deliberately untouched (guests should appear in admin lists); Create/ImportCSV use `provision_account(...,p_create_only=>TRUE)`, which refuses every existing stable identity before any update, and the alias/quota verbs 404 naturally on the guest's missing primary mailbox. DB-gated proof: `admin_guest_target_test.go`, `admin_users_test.go`. G2a supplies the dedicated lifecycle surface.

**Guests** (`admin_guests.go`, `admin_guest_profiles.go`; Guest Accounts G1 + G2a + G5) — invite, lifecycle, profile, pending-invite revoke, invite-domain, and sponsor-transfer administration:
- `POST /admin/guests` — strict `{email, displayName, expiresAt?, profileId?}` (`password` is rejected) on `workerPool`. One transaction performs the fail-closed domain precheck → optional `guest_profile_invite_snapshot` (authorized default-expiry read plus retained profile `FOR SHARE`) → `provision_account(..., p_guest=>TRUE)` → optional `guest_profile_apply` of that serialized profile version → authoritative satellite/creator/config lookup → token mint/invite insert; any pre-commit failure rolls provisioning back. A concurrent profile edit either wins before the snapshot or waits for invite commit, so default expiry and policy cannot mix versions. Explicit expiry wins over the profile default. The raw token never crosses SQL; mail sends only after commit and a send failure leaves the pending invite. Response remains exactly `{guestUserId, invitedEmail, expiresAt}`.
- `DELETE /admin/guests/{userId}/invite` requires `{expectedInviteId:"<decimal>"}` and calls the authoritative compare-and-set reducer. The handler runs an explicit `admin_can_reach(actor,guest)` UX preflight inside its transaction so the cross-tenant reach-coverage guard sees the boundary and returns the uniform 403 early. This preflight is not mutation authority: the reducer locks the guest/user rows, resolves immutable guest ownership, and repeats live `guests.manage` reach in every mutation predicate, closing TOCTOU. It returns `revoked`→200, no current live invite→404, or replacement/id mismatch→409 after commit; clients refresh on 404/409. `GET /admin/guests/{id}` projects the exact current invite plus the latest 10 history rows, a `hasMore` signal, decimal-string ids, derived natural expiry, legacy `unknown`, and creator email only when the reader can currently reach that creator. `POST /admin/guest-invite-domains` and `DELETE /admin/guest-invite-domains/{domain}` still route writes through `guest_invite_domain_set`; all guest routes remain feature-gated.
- Initial invite and re-invite responses include the exact decimal-string `inviteId`. A post-commit mail failure is the structured retryable 503 `invite_delivery_unconfirmed`; the committed invite remains pending, raw token/address never enter the error, and callers must refresh or explicitly use re-invite rather than automatically replay the initial create.
- G2a routes `GET /admin/guests[/{id}]`, `POST /admin/guests/{id}/{disable|enable|extend}`, `PUT /admin/guests/{id}/profile`, `GET /admin/guest-profile-domains`, and `GET|POST|PUT|DELETE /admin/guest-profiles[/{id}]` through the worker pool. Reads accept `guests.view` or `guests.manage`; writes require `guests.manage`; an outer feature gate returns 404 before the audited permission path. Because this is a BYPASSRLS pool, List/Get row predicates independently require `guest_admin_can_reach_with_permission(actor,target,'guests.view') OR ...'guests.manage'`, and the status prelock requires its `'guests.manage'` arm: JWT claims cannot outlive a revoked persisted row or compose permission from one assignment with reach from another. `guest-profile-domains` returns only `{id,name}` for local domains covered by a live unscoped assignment carrying the required guest permission. Profile booleans are pointer-decoded; `apps` and `defaultExpiryDays` are presence-aware raw fields. Every fixed boolean, `apps`, and `defaultExpiryDays` must be present; literal `false`, `apps:[]`, and `defaultExpiryDays:null` are valid, while omitted/null/non-array `apps` is HTTP 400. Profile reads/writes require the same unscoped domain authority; create locks the qualifying local domain `FOR SHARE` and repeats existence/local/authority in the INSERT, predicate-bearing mutations reject zero-row races as BZ170, and name collisions are BZ224. Lifecycle/profile-assignment writes couple `guests.manage`, immutable host-domain reach, and optional OU/set reach on one assignment. `enable` is only disabled→active; `extend` accepts active, disabled, or expired with a future expiry (including an active row whose stored expiry has elapsed), while invited remains rejected so renewal cannot bypass acceptance. Missing/out-of-scope targets remain uniform BZ170.
- G5 adds `GET /admin/guests/{id}/sponsor-options` and `POST /admin/guests/{id}/transfer`. Candidate selection and the reducer require one live `guests.manage` assignment to reach both the guest and enabled non-guest sponsor; transfer locks `guest_accounts` before either `users` row, matching lifecycle order, then atomically updates sponsorship plus guest-owned Chat/Drive authority without rewriting attribution. List/detail expose `sponsorNeedsReview` for missing, disabled, or guest sponsors. Disable commits the reducer-owned security transition first, then makes a bounded immediate `guestlifecycle.DrainUser` attempt; a LiveKit failure never rolls back the disable because the durable outbox worker converges independently.
- G2d's `TestGuestG2Tracer` composes the real admin and Chat handlers with the
  reducers through invite/accept, profile assignment and derived Chat
  entitlement, membership policy/floor, live read-only BZ220, expire/extend,
  disable/enable clean slate, and re-invite identity reuse. The database Chat
  toggle is enabled only inside the test and restored on cleanup; the product
  feature remains seeded OFF. This is the lifecycle half of the composite G2d
  gate; the independent `chat_test.TestGuestEventSourceChatFrame` owns the real
  EventSource transport half described in the `ws` section above.

**Member guest-invite approvals** (`approval/guest_invite.go`, approval-engine consumer #8, Guest G1 Task 7):
`POST /me/guest-invites` strictly accepts `{invitedEmail,message?}`, hides from
guest principals, and returns 404 unless BOTH `feature.guest_accounts` and
`approval.guest_invite` are enabled; a missing policy is 422. `Decide` first
does the normal `RunUserScoped` visibility read, then routes `guest.invite` to
the worker pool. Approval decision, approved-only guest provisioning, token-hash
creation, and pending invite insertion share one transaction; email is after
commit, and a transport failure is sanitized in logs while the invite remains
pending. Reject provisions nothing. `approvalItem` exposes payload only for the
existing HR forms and the safe guest evidence allowlist—mail replay payloads
remain withheld. `NewHandler`'s signature is unchanged; `SetGuestInviteDelivery`
wires the production MinIO/bucket transport.

**Domains** (`admin_domains.go`):
- `GET/POST/GET/{id}/PUT/{id}/DELETE/{id} /admin/domains` — Domain CRUD; MX/SPF/DKIM DNS record verification

**Mailboxes** (`admin_mailboxes.go`):
- `GET/POST/GET/{id}/PUT/{id}/DELETE/{id} /admin/mailboxes` — Mailbox CRUD (personal, distribution, shared types)
- `GET/POST/PUT/DELETE /admin/mailboxes/{id}/folders` — Folder management per mailbox
- `POST /admin/mailboxes/{id}/import` — Mailbox import (EML, PST, ICS, VCF)
- `GET /admin/mailboxes/{id}/export` — Mailbox export

**Rules** (`admin/` + `user/rules.go` + `user/user_rules.go`):
- `GET/POST/GET/{id}/PUT/{id}/DELETE/{id}/POST/{id}/test /admin/rules` — Admin rule CRUD + test execution (RulesHandler; List/Get/Create are intentionally UNSCOPED cross-tenant admin, so they are admin-perm-gated and must NOT back a user route). Runs on workerPool (BYPASSRLS) for the cross-tenant Test().
- `GET/POST /settings/rules`, `PUT/DELETE /settings/rules/{id}` — END-USER mail-filter CRUD (`UserRulesHandler`, requireAuth). The security-scoped counterpart: EVERY query is constrained to the caller's own mailboxes AND `is_transport_rule=FALSE` (List/Get/Update/Delete carry the ownership WHERE; Create forces `mailbox_id=ResolvePrimaryMailbox(auth)` + `is_transport_rule=FALSE`, ignoring any client `mailboxId`/`isTransportRule`). App pool (own rows only). Reuses the `rules` engine (apply_rules/eval_conditions/apply_action) unchanged — no reducer/schema change. Proven by `user_rules_test.go` (cross-tenant read/write blocked; transport rules invisible/untouchable). The webmail builder UI is a follow-up.
- `GET/POST/GET/{id}/PUT/{id}/DELETE/{id} /admin/rule-templates` — Rule template management with visibility control

**DLP** (`admin_dlp.go`):
- `GET/PUT /admin/dlp/patterns` — Data Loss Prevention regex pattern management

**Quarantine** (`admin_quarantine.go`):
- `GET/stats/preview/release/reject /admin/quarantine` — Admin quarantine management. **Cross-tenant** (W7): the org-wide `requireMailPerm` is the only ownership gate — List/Preview/Release/Reject act on EVERY mailbox's messages, with NO per-user predicate (a self-scope predicate here silently no-op'd any release/reject of another user's message). `Release`/`Reject` fold the `released_at IS NULL` precondition into the mutating `UPDATE … RETURNING` and 404 on 0 rows, so a wrong or already-processed id cannot report false success.
- `GET/count/release/reject /quarantine` — User self-service quarantine

**Retention** (`admin_retention.go`):

**System** (`admin_system.go`, `admin_upgrade.go`):
- `GET /admin/system/health` — System health aggregate
- `POST/GET/download/DELETE /admin/system/backup` — On-demand backup management
- `GET /admin/system/migrations` — DB migration status
- `GET /admin/system/version` — Build version
- `GET /admin/system/status` — Component status (DB, S3, SMTP, IMAP, LDAP)
- `GET /admin/system/upgrade/status` — read-only legacy upgrade history
- `POST /admin/system/upgrade/{check,apply,rollback}` — U-1 compatibility interlock: always return typed RFC 7807 `410 Gone` before decoding input or performing filesystem/DB mutation; the gateway must never recover self-update authority
- `GET/PUT /admin/system/auto-update` — retired path-based auto-update compatibility shell; typed RFC 7807 `410 Gone` before parsing or persistence
- `GET/PUT /admin/system/maintenance-window` — retired split maintenance-window compatibility shell; typed RFC 7807 `410 Gone`; scheduling is part of signed release intent creation
- `POST /admin/reload` — Config hot reload via HTTP

**Features** (`admin_features.go`):
- `GET /admin/features` — list compiled definitions plus stored/effective/source state, classified unknown rows, and additive shared operator metadata (`changeMode`, `disableBehavior`, non-null `dependencies`, `confirmationRequired`). Metadata does not alter `Definition`/`RegistryDigest`; unknown or unannotated rows carry empty axes and no warning.
- `PUT /admin/features/{key}` — audited reducer-authoritative known/observe-compatible mutation; enforce rejects unknown keys. Registered legacy keys such as `device_telemetry` remain writable even though unknown compatibility keys must use dotted lowercase segments.
- `DELETE /admin/features/{key}` — audited unknown-only cleanup in observe or enforce; known keys are immutable through this route
- `PUT /admin/features/registry-mode` — audited observe/enforce transition; enforce atomically requires digest parity and zero unknown rows, while observe remains a recovery path under skew

**Auth methods** (`admin_auth.go`):
- `GET/PUT /admin/auth/methods` — List and configure auth methods (Basic, Kerberos, SAML, OIDC)

**Identity** (`admin_identity.go`):
- `GET/PUT /admin/identity/group-mappings` — Map SAML/OIDC group claims to roles
- `GET /admin/identity/inventory` — aggregate-only Identity Modernization I0 inventory on `workerPool`: people/account-class/mailbox/source counts, complete/partial federation-pair posture, protected-account totals, V153 ambiguity, and AD/LDAP secret posture. It returns no user IDs, addresses, external subjects, config values, or ciphertext. Stable blockers are shared with `mailctl identity inventory`, which exits non-zero when any remain. The protected set is the union of CA break-glass users and holders of a live role containing a canonical `permissions.SensitivePermissions` key; full class rules live in `docs/standards/IDENTITY_ACCOUNT_CLASSES.md`. Group-mapping writes use a 1 MiB body cap and shared validation: no more than 100 mappings, each non-empty, control-free, and at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes.
- `admin_identity_source.go` owns normalized source management: plan/apply/detail/export, raw-byte secret set/rotation, zero-person-write dry-run, and explicit activate/pause/resume/retire. Documents are strict 64 KiB `setjoin.io/v1` JSON with a closed `local|ad|ldap|scim|saml|oidc` discriminator; AD and OIDC accept only hard immutable subjects. Every source mutation is unscoped-admin only, binds body/header UUID+reason exactly, and uses a strong generation `If-Match`. The gateway encrypts the entire canonical document+secret map with exact `identity_source.<sourceKey>` AAD before SQL; plaintext secret input is accepted only as bounded `application/octet-stream`, cleared after use, and never returned or logged. Dry-run checks schema and the kind-specific secret catalog, then performs the configured AD/LDAP bind plus bounded search through the production connector; raw TCP reachability alone is never validation. Runtime documents decode stored Base64 secret bytes before connector construction and fail closed on malformed encoding. The source catalog returns healthy runnable sources plus isolated per-source diagnostics, so one malformed active legacy source cannot crash-loop the gateway or suppress unrelated healthy sources. Periodic AD/LDAP cycles log returned sync failures. A successful dry-run records `validated`, always reports `personWrites: 0`, and performs no person writes.

**Roles** (`admin_roles.go`) — all routes gated by `roles.manage` (NOT the Auth console's `users.read`): `roles.manage` is the only permission that lets a principal mint/grant arbitrary permission sets, so a weaker guard would let a `user_admin` self-escalate to super-admin. Built-in roles are immutable (409 on PUT/DELETE). Pinned by `roles_guard_test.go`. Hosted in the **Access console** (5th console, `access` ConsoleID, `roles.manage`-gated) at `/admin/roles`.
- `GET/POST/GET/{id}/PUT/{id}/DELETE/{id} /admin/roles` — Custom role CRUD
- `POST/DELETE /admin/roles/{id}/members` — Role membership management

**Access Reviews** (`access_reviews.go`, `AccessReviewsHandler`) — all 6 routes gated by `roles.manage`; domain-scope-confined (each campaign snapshots only the actor's scoped assignments). Thin translators: each method reads claims → calls one reducer → maps BZ codes to HTTP. BZ060→404 (not found: campaign or item), BZ061→403 (out of scope), BZ062→409 (campaign closed), BZ063→409 (SSO-revoke blocked), BZ069→403 (self-certification — an actor may not decide their own access; SoD). Empty-name validation is Go-side (HTTP 400) — `access_review_start` raises no BZ code.
- `GET /admin/access-reviews` — List campaigns
- `POST /admin/access-reviews` — Start campaign (`access_review_start`)
- `GET /admin/access-reviews/{id}` — Get campaign + items
- `PUT /admin/access-reviews/{id}/items/{itemId}` — Decide single item (`access_review_decide`)
- `PUT /admin/access-reviews/{id}/items` — Bulk decide all undecided items (`access_review_decide_bulk`)
- `POST /admin/access-reviews/{id}/close` — Close + apply revocations (`access_review_close`)

**Permissions catalog** (`admin_permissions.go`): `GET /admin/permissions` — the 23-permission catalog (key/category/description/isSensitive) for the role editor UI. Gated `roles.manage`.

**Effective permissions**: `GET /admin/users/{id}/effective-permissions` — resolved union + narrowest scope + per-role sources (via permissions.Resolve). Gated `roles.manage`; honors domain scope.

**Conditional access** (`admin_conditional_access.go`, `admin_named_locations.go`, `admin_auth_strengths.go`, `admin_ca_templates.go`, `admin_ca_why.go`, `admin_ca_impact.go`, `admin_ca_simulate.go`, `admin_ca_coverage.go`, `admin_ca_import_export.go`):
- `GET/POST/PUT/{id}/DELETE/{id}/reorder /admin/conditional-access/policies` — Conditional access policy CRUD
- `GET /admin/conditional-access/why?user=<id|email>&since=<24h|7d|30d>[&correlation_id=]` — Why-blocked trace (CA-1 D5): per-user evaluated[] trace from ca_signin_log; user-not-found → 404 {found:false}, found-zero-rows → 200 {found:true,rows:[]}
- `GET /admin/conditional-access/policies/{id}/impact?window=<24h|7d|30d>[&list=blocked_users]` — Policy impact (CA-1 D5): aggregate counts + blocked-user list via ca_policy_impact; passes through window_has_data
- `POST /admin/conditional-access/simulate` — What-If simulator (CA-1 D6): read-only hypothetical evaluation via engine.SimulateWithPolicies; no enforcement, no audit
- `GET /admin/conditional-access/coverage` — Coverage report (CA-1 D7): gap check + shadow detector via ca_coverage_report(); always returns {gaps:[],shadowed:[]} (never null arrays)
- `GET /admin/conditional-access/export` — Export live policies + named_locations + auth_strengths as a policy document ({schemaVersion, generatedBy, policies, named_locations, auth_strengths})
- `POST /admin/conditional-access/import?mode=merge|replace|prune` — Bulk import via ca_policy_import; BZ088/090/092→422, BZ091→404; import born report_only
- `POST /admin/conditional-access/diff` — Structured add/change/remove plan (server-side, no mutation); change detection normalizes via json.Compact to avoid whitespace false-positives
- Named locations, auth strengths, templates CRUD (CA-1 D1/D4)

**AD Sync** (`admin_ad.go`):
- `GET/PUT /admin/ad/config` — AD sync configuration. `PUT` accepts at most 64 string values totaling the 1 MiB body cap and rejects every key outside `ad_sync_`. `_enc` values route through the identity `ConfigStore` EK01 codec before PostgreSQL sees them, then `ReloadConfig` decrypts the stored config and atomically swaps the guarded worker pointer. The side-effect hook remains asynchronous; `GET` always redacts secrets to `[ENCRYPTED]`.
- `POST /admin/ad/test-connection` — Connection test
- `POST /admin/ad/sync` — Trigger manual sync
- `GET /admin/ad/sync-log` — Sync log
- `GET /admin/ad/health` — AD health
- `GET/POST/{id}/resolve/resolve-all /admin/ad/conflicts` — Identity conflict management
- `GET/PUT /admin/ad/field-policy` — Per-field AD authority policy

**LDAP sync** (`admin_ldap.go`):
- `GET/PUT /admin/ldap/config`, `test-connection`, `GET preview`, `sync`, `sync-log`, `health` — LDAP identity sync management. `PUT /config` accepts at most 64 string values under the 1 MiB body cap, rejects keys outside `ldap_sync_`, encrypts `_enc` values through the identity `ConfigStore`, fires the async hook, and synchronously `ReloadConfig`s a worker from decrypted runtime values. AD/LDAP/TLS config reads pass non-secret JSONB strings through `configStringForResponse` so consoles receive the exact scalar rather than recursively persisting JSON quotes; secret rows are redacted before decoding. The worker pointer and enabled state are mutex-guarded; console-only setup survives restart. A valid disabled configuration retains its worker only for the **true dry-run** `TestConnection` (`ldapsync.SyncWorker.DryRun` — bind + search first 5 + count, ZERO writes) and read-only `GET preview?limit=N`; it does not start a periodic worker, the config side-effect does not call `SyncNow`, manual sync returns 503, and `SyncWorker.sync` rechecks the live enabled resolver before any directory or database access. Preview maps up to N directory entries to their would-be `create/update/disable/skip` action via read-only DB lookups plus a `total` count. Directory errors are classified `network` vs `bind_auth`. `GET /config` redacts secret rows. `ldap_sync_enabled` remains a plain `system_config` toggle; the console gates enabling it behind confirmation.

**Identity connector secret boundary** (`admin/config_secret.go`, `admin/config_store.go`, `cmd/mailserverd/main.go`):
- AD/LDAP `_enc` JSON values are stored as `setjoin-config-secret` records carrying an EK01 AES-256-GCM/SM4-GCM envelope, explicit key version, and legacy source key. AAD binds deployment ID + source key + config key + key version. New writes use the active key; reads accept only the active or explicitly configured retiring versions. `IDENTITY_CONFIG_SECRET_KEY` defaults to `DB_ENCRYPTION_KEY`; version defaults to 1; `IDENTITY_CONFIG_SECRET_RETIRING_KEYS` is a JSON version→key map.
- Startup loads the durable V154 `identity.deployment_id`, transactionally upgrades legacy AD/LDAP plaintext, validates every existing envelope, and exits before constructing stored-config workers if a key/version/AAD is wrong. Validation reports every invalid config key in one pass and performs no plaintext upgrades until all envelopes authenticate. Because AEAD authentication cannot distinguish a wrong key, altered ciphertext, or mismatched AAD, the startup error directs recovery to verify active/retiring keys and restore `identity.deployment_id` together with the ciphertext; it must not claim a synthetic deployment-mismatch diagnosis or attempt an unauthenticated rewrap. `[ENCRYPTED]` is preserve-only and cannot create a missing secret. Admin reads/exports remain redacted; full database backup/restore preserves the ciphertext and deployment ID together.
